
DoloGram
A Personal, Free-to-Build Messenger App
Product & Technical Reference Document (v14)
Stack: React Native (Expo) · Next.js · Supabase
Conventions: Assumption: marks a claim that could not be verified. N/A: marks something that does not apply. OD-nn refers to Section 8 (Open decisions). D-nn refers to Section 9 (Decision log). LS-n refers to a Low Signal Mode requirement in Section 3.4. MUST, SHOULD and MAY have the meaning defined in RFC 2119. Unless a statement uses SHOULD or MAY, a present-tense statement about DoloGram in Sections 3 to 6 is a requirement equivalent to MUST. Section 10 (Implementation Plan) adds the identifiers M0 to M6, Mn-nn, PF-n and PR-n. Versions 4 to 6 close OD-01 to OD-23 (OD-09 was already closed in v3): each is now cited by its decision number, D-21 to D-49, and Section 8 has no open decisions. Version 6 also closes the residual items of v5 as D-56 to D-65, and version 7 records that builds use EAS Build only (D-66). Version 8 specifies V2 and V3 and closes the deferred items, decision-log gaps and ambiguities of v7 as D-67 to D-84. Version 9 closes what v8 left conditional, the remaining decision-log gaps and the remaining ambiguities as D-85 to D-101. Version 10 adds the owner's admin web (Section 6.6) as D-102 to D-108. Version 11 adds Section 11, the screen-by-screen breakdown of the three clients, with the screen IDs AND-nn (Android), WEB-nn (web) and ADM-nn (admin) and the UI rules of D-109. Version 12 closes the last conditional item (D-108), the decision-log gaps and the ambiguities of v11 as D-110 to D-126, and records the claims checked against the Supabase documentation (D-125). Version 13 adds Section 12, the design system of the three clients, with the component identifiers CMP-nn (CMP-01 to CMP-31) and the decisions D-127 to D-140; token names are written as dot names, for example color.bg.app. Version 14 makes Instagram the main UI reference of Section 12, adds the components CMP-32 to CMP-34 and the decisions D-141 to D-146, and updates the navigation of 11.2 and 11.3 to a bottom tab bar and a web navigation rail.

1. Core Product Purpose
DoloGram is a personal-use messaging application inspired by Telegram's features, built with free-tier tools and services. It targets Android (Expo) and the web (Next.js), is not distributed through Google Play or the App Store, and has no commercial or production-scale requirements. It is used by its creator and a small circle of contacts, usually 2 to 10 people, and is designed to run unchanged up to the Supabase Free plan limits (D-41).
1.1 Goals
Replicate the core Telegram experience: cloud-synced, multi-device chat with media support, limited to the features in Section 3.2.
Stay free to operate: every in-scope feature runs on free tiers of Supabase, Expo and the services named in Section 6, with no recurring paid API costs. Building and installing the apps is also free: there is no store account, and no paid email or SMS service (D-47, D-53).
Work on weak connections: Low Signal Mode (Section 3.4) queues messages while the device is offline and sends them when a connection returns; on 2G/3G it compresses photos and defers large media.
One backend for both clients: a single Supabase project serves the Expo Android app and the Next.js web app, and the owner runs it from a local admin web (Section 6.6).
1.2 Non-Goals
Public distribution, store compliance, or monetization.
Telegram-scale infrastructure: capacity beyond the Supabase Free plan limits listed in Section 7.1.
Paid third-party APIs (speech-to-text, paid CDN, SMS providers).
A bot platform or automation framework.
1.3 Target User
The owner and a small, trusted group of contacts who want a private messenger with Telegram-style chat and media handling that stays usable on unreliable mobile networks. Typical use is 2 to 10 people; the supported ceiling is the Free plan limits in Section 7.1 (D-41).
2. Telegram Feature Catalog
Reference list of Telegram features by category. It does not define scope: Section 3.2 lists what DoloGram builds and Section 3.3 lists what it excludes. Where a Section 3.2 row names a variant (for example static location sharing), only that variant is in scope. Catalog items with no row in either section are out of scope and listed in D-80.

3. Scope and Requirements
This section defines what DoloGram implements, by phase, and the mechanism for each feature.
3.1 Build Phases
MVP: the core usable messenger; complete before V2 starts; complete means every test in Section 7.2 passes.
V2: features that add depth but are not required for daily use.
V3 (stretch): calls; started only after MVP is complete and V2 passes its acceptance tests (Section 7.4, D-67). A test that a D-85 fallback makes not applicable counts as passed for its gate when the test log records the fallback (D-121).
Implementation: Section 10.3 gives the milestones that build each phase, with entry and exit conditions.
3.2 Feature-to-Mechanism Map
| Feature | Phase | Mechanism |
| 1-on-1 & group chat | MVP | Supabase Realtime Postgres Changes on messages (Section 6.4). Finding users, creating chats and managing members: D-26, D-70, D-91. |
| Text formatting | MVP | Markdown only; HTML is not rendered (D-05). Mobile: react-native-markdown-display (published on npm; renders Markdown with React Native components using markdown-it). Assumption: it works with the Expo SDK in use; checked by the M2-06 spike, fallback react-native-marked (D-85 item 1). Web: react-markdown, whose documentation says raw HTML is escaped or ignored by default. Assumption: the web client shows HTML tags as literal text instead of dropping them; checked by T-12, fallback an HTML-node-to-text handler (D-85 item 2). Supported syntax: D-30; constructs outside it: D-99; character counting: D-116. |
| Message edit / delete | MVP | UPDATE of content (edit) or deleted_at (soft delete) on messages; sender only; the server sets edited_at and deleted_at and clears the content on delete. Rules: D-22, D-76. |
| Reply | MVP | reply_to column referencing the parent message. |
| Forward | MVP | New message in the target chat with the copied text; a message with attachments forwards its text only (D-50). Attribution: D-31. |
| Reactions | MVP | reactions table (message_id, chat_id, user_id, emoji, removed_at); a reaction is removed by UPDATE of removed_at, never by DELETE. Rules: D-22. |
| Read receipts | MVP | Persisted read state: chat_members.last_read_seq, with a Broadcast read hint so an open chat updates at once. Rules: D-23, D-92. |
| Typing indicators | MVP | Realtime Broadcast only (ephemeral, not stored, advisory: D-92); Presence is not used; display rule: D-118. |
| Photos, videos, voice notes, files | MVP | Supabase Storage private buckets by size class, with signed URLs. Limits: D-29, D-75, in bytes per D-116; signed URLs last 10 minutes (D-113); attachments per message and object checks: D-90. |
| Saved Messages | MVP | Direct chat whose only member is the user. |
| Folders, archive, mute | MVP | Per-user settings synced across devices. Storage: D-24; list, archive and folder rules: D-94. |
| Dark mode | MVP | Light and dark themes on both clients. Selection mechanism: D-35, D-83. |
| Low Signal Mode | MVP | Section 3.4 (behavior) and Section 6.3 (implementation). |
| Admin web (owner only) | MVP | Local Next.js app on 127.0.0.1:3100, never deployed, with the secret key in server code only: accounts, premium flags (V2), health checks and maintenance jobs, and no access to message content. Section 6.6; D-102 to D-108; account deletion erases and disables the account (D-110). |
| Push notifications | V2 | Expo Notifications on Android. Phase and sender mechanism: D-25; recipients and webhook authorization: D-96. |
| App lock (optional) | V2 | Local authentication on mobile (Section 5.1). Phase: D-21. |
| Stickers & GIFs (custom only) | V2 | Static asset bundle; no external GIF API (D-59, D-67); GIFs are sent as stickers (D-120); premium stickers: D-97. |
| Polls | V2 | polls and poll_votes tables (D-59, D-67); a poll is sent through send_poll (D-120). |
| Location sharing (static) | V2 | Device GPS; the message is a card with the coordinates and an "Open in Maps" link, with no map SDK (D-49). |
| Secret Chats (E2EE) | V2 | Client-side libsodium encryption (Section 4). Key management: D-33, D-49; scope: D-68; membership and server checks: D-90, D-91. |
| Self-destructing messages | V2 | ttl_seconds and expires_at columns (Section 6.2) plus a pg_cron cleanup every minute; attachments become unreadable at expiry (D-69); the cleanup is the only update allowed after expiry (D-111). |
| Admin roles & group permissions | V2 | role and restricted_until columns on chat_members (D-71); what a restricted member may still do: D-112. |
| Premium cosmetic features | V2 | Flags on profiles (premium_flags). Scope: D-32, D-72; flag visibility: D-87; extra-reaction enforcement: D-119. |
| Voice / video calls | V3 | WebRTC, 1-on-1, with Google's public STUN server and no TURN relay. Scope and provider: D-34, D-73; invitations: D-98. |
---TABLE END---

3.3 Explicitly Excluded
Bots and automation.
Public/global discovery of channels.
Live streaming and screen sharing.
Faster download speeds (Telegram's CDN tier).
Unlimited voice-to-text transcription.
Rationale: D-15.
3.4 Low Signal Mode
Low Signal Mode keeps messaging usable on weak or absent connections. It has two triggers: automatic detection of a 2G/3G cellular connection, and a user toggle named "Force Low Signal Mode".
LS-1 Local-first. Every outgoing message, with or without attachments, is written to a device-local queue before any network call and appears in the chat with status queued.
LS-2 Status. Each outgoing item shows exactly one of queued, sending, sent, failed. Two labels refine these states and are not additional states (D-17): "sending (low signal)" on sending while Low Signal Mode is active, and "waiting for Wi-Fi" on queued while the item is deferred under LS-5.
LS-3 Delivery worker. While the app, or the browser tab, is in the foreground, the worker attempts delivery on every connectivity-change event and on a timer (interval: 15 seconds, D-27) whenever the device reports any connection. It does not run in the background; queued items send on the next open (D-109, D-117).
LS-4 Text first. The text of a queued item is delivered before its attachments: the message row, with its attachment_count (D-90), is inserted first, and each attachment is then uploaded and linked. The item becomes sent only after all attachments are uploaded; until then other members see one placeholder per missing attachment. A deferred or slow upload never delays later items of the same chat, and an interrupted upload restarts from the beginning (D-93).
LS-5 Behavior matrix.
| Condition | Text | Photos | Video, voice notes, files |
| Normal: Wi-Fi, 4G or 5G, toggle off | Sent immediately | Sent without Low Signal compression | Sent immediately |
| Automatic: cellular 2G or 3G, toggle off | Sent immediately | Compressed, then sent immediately | Items above the large-media threshold wait for Wi-Fi; smaller items sent immediately |
| Forced: toggle on, any network | Sent immediately | Compressed, then wait for Wi-Fi | Wait for Wi-Fi |
| No connection | Queued | Queued | Queued |
---TABLE END---
The automatic trigger is cellular generation 2G or 3G as reported by the device (D-13); a generation the device cannot determine is treated as not 2G or 3G, and a connection that is neither Wi-Fi nor cellular (for example ethernet or unknown) counts as Normal (D-93). There is no latency-based trigger. The large-media threshold is 5 MB (5,242,880 bytes, D-116). Compression outputs JPEG at quality 0.7 with the long edge scaled down to at most 1280 px; a smaller image is re-encoded and never enlarged, and a compressed photo keeps its compressed file on every later attempt (D-28, D-117). The row is evaluated at each delivery attempt from the network state at that moment (D-117). "No connection" overrides every other row. "Wait for Wi-Fi" means the item is sent as soon as the device reports a Wi-Fi connection while the app is in the foreground, and immediately if it is already on Wi-Fi (D-109, D-117).
LS-6 Toggle scope. Changing the toggle applies to items queued after the change: each item records the toggle at enqueue, and the network row of LS-5 is evaluated at each attempt (D-117).
LS-7 Retry. A failed delivery attempt is retried with exponential backoff (base 2 s, factor 2, cap 60 s, jitter ±20%, at most 8 attempts: D-27). An attempt counts toward the 8 only when the device reported a connection and the request failed. The timer and connectivity events wake the worker but never start an attempt before the item's backoff has elapsed; regaining a connection makes every queued item due at once without resetting its count. A rate-limit rejection (Section 5.5) is not an attempt: the item waits for the retry time the server returns (D-77). An item that exhausts its retries becomes failed and stays visible; nothing fails silently. A failed item offers manual Retry, which sets retry_count to 0 and makes the item due at once, and Discard. Discard removes an item whose message row the server never stored; when the row exists, Discard soft-deletes it first, so it needs a connection and is disabled offline (D-93). The queue holds at most 200 items that are not yet sent (queued, sending or failed); a new item beyond that is refused with a visible message. The message rows of one chat are inserted strictly in enqueue order; attachment uploads run after their row is stored and may finish in any order (D-27, D-93).
LS-8 Idempotency. Each queue item carries a client-generated message_id (D-10), and the server accepts at most one message per message_id, so retries never create duplicates. A retry rejected because that message_id already exists counts as delivered, and the item becomes sent.
LS-9 Persistence. The queue survives app termination and restart. On startup, items left in sending are returned to queued and retried.
LS-10 Web. The web client queues in IndexedDB and retries when the browser reports it is online and on a timer (D-12). Compression and Wi-Fi deferral are mobile-only (D-19).
3.5 MVP Flow Requirements
Per-flow behavior for every MVP user-facing flow (F1 to F9) and for F10 and F11, which are V2 (D-21, D-25), and F12, the owner's admin web (D-102). Behavior that no existing code or document defines points to the decision that settles it.
| Flow | Requirements |
| F1 Sign-in and session | Success: email and password sign-in creates a session and opens the chat list. The session persists across app restarts and refreshes automatically (Section 5.1). A signed-in user can sign out and change their password from settings (D-51); a new account must change its first password at first sign-in and can read nothing until it does (D-60, D-78). / Empty: N/A: no list is shown before sign-in. / Offline: sign-in fails with an error and creates no session. With an existing session the app opens to cached chat history (Section 6.1). / Permission-denied: a wrong email or password creates no session. Only the owner creates accounts (D-47). / Validation: the email must be a syntactically valid address and a new password has at least 12 characters (D-47). Supabase rate-limits repeated sign-in attempts. Assumption: the project's default Auth rate limits apply; M0-06 reads and records the configured values (D-84); fallback: if no sign-in limit is configured, M0-06 sets one of 30 requests per 5 minutes per IP address in the Auth rate-limit settings (D-85 item 17). Nothing is sent by email. |
| F2 Chat list | Success: lists the chats the user belongs to that are not archived, most recent message first by messages.created_at; a chat with no messages sorts by chats.created_at, and ties sort by chat_id (D-94). / Empty: N/A: Saved Messages is always listed; the trigger that creates the profile creates it (M1-03, D-70). / Offline: shows the cached chat list from local persistence. / Permission-denied: lists only chats where the user has a chat_members row (Section 5.2). / Validation: archive, mute and folder rules: D-24. |
| F3 Open chat, send text, reply, forward, react | Success: a sent message appears in the sender's chat at once with a status per LS-2, and on every other member's client without a manual refresh. A reply stores reply_to. A forward copies content into the target chat. A reaction added or removed by its own user is visible to all members. / Empty: a chat with no messages shows no message rows; wording: D-35. / Offline: the message is queued (LS-1) and sent once on reconnection (LS-8). Reactions while offline: D-22. / Permission-denied: an insert as another user, or into a chat the user is not a member of, is rejected (Section 5.2). / Validation: text is rendered as Markdown; HTML tags appear as literal text (D-05). Length and empty-message rules: D-30, enforced on the server by D-90. A reply_to must name a message of the same chat (D-90). Reply to a deleted message: D-22. |
| F4 Edit and delete | Success: when the sender edits a message, edited_at is set and all members see the new content in real time. When the sender deletes it, deleted_at is set and no member sees its content; display form and whether the stored content is cleared: D-22. / Empty: N/A: acts on an existing message. / Offline: D-22. / Permission-denied: an update by anyone other than the sender is rejected (Section 5.2). / Validation: time limit, delete-for-self and admin delete: D-22. |
| F5 Send photo, video, voice note or file | Success: the attachment is uploaded to Storage, a message_media row links it to its message, and members see it. Delivery follows LS-5. / Empty: N/A: no list state. / Offline: the item is queued (LS-1) and uploaded after reconnection; an app restart does not lose it (LS-9). / Permission-denied: Storage policy rejects upload to, or download from, a chat the user is not a member of (Section 5.3). If the OS denies camera, microphone or media access, the action does not start; message: D-35. / Validation: type and size are checked on the client and by the Storage bucket limits (Section 5.5); limits: D-29, D-75, in bytes per D-116; at most 10 attachments per message, each bound to its own uploaded object (D-90). The platform ceiling is 50 MB per file on the Supabase Free plan (Section 7.1). |
| F6 Read receipts and typing indicators | Success: other members see a typing indicator while the user types; it names one typist or counts several and hides 5 seconds after the last event or when the typist's message arrives (D-57, D-118). Read status is shown on messages per D-23 and, in an open chat, updates within 2 seconds of the reader's update (D-92). / Empty: N/A: no list state. / Offline: typing is not sent while offline. Read-state updates while offline: D-23. / Permission-denied: only chat members receive that chat's typing events (Section 6.4). / Validation: N/A: no user input. |
| F7 Saved Messages | All states as F3, in a direct chat whose only member is the user. Forwarding into it follows D-31. The representation is fixed by D-70. |
| F8 Low Signal Mode | Success: LS-1 to LS-10. / Empty: N/A: an empty queue means every item is sent. / Offline: LS-1 and LS-3. / Permission-denied: N/A: no user permission is involved. / Validation: a send that fails mid-flight, including after a network change, follows LS-7. The toggle state persists across restarts as a device preference in AsyncStorage (D-83, D-100). |
| F9 Chat organization and theme | Success: a user archives, mutes or files a chat in a folder; the change persists and appears on the user's other devices (D-24). All screens render in light and dark themes. / Empty: empty folder or archive wording: D-35. / Offline: the controls are disabled, because changes need a connection (D-24). / Permission-denied: a user changes only their own settings; RLS limits chat_members updates and folders to the caller's own rows (10.5). / Validation: folder name and count limits: D-24, enforced by a CHECK and a trigger (D-94). |
| F10 App lock (mobile; phase: D-21) | Success: with the lock enabled, the chat list is not shown until local authentication succeeds. / Offline: works offline; it is a local gate independent of the network session (Section 5.1). / Permission-denied: failed authentication keeps the app locked. A device with no enrolled biometrics or device credential cannot enable the lock: the app checks enrollment through expo-local-authentication before turning it on (D-100). / Empty / Validation: N/A: no list or input. / Web: N/A: the lock uses a mobile-only module. |
| F11 Push notifications (Android) | Phase, sender mechanism, token storage and payload content: D-25. Recipients, title and webhook authorization: D-96. The app registers a push token only after the OS notification permission is granted; if it is denied, no token row exists, so no alert is sent and the app is otherwise unaffected (D-96). |
| F12 Admin web (owner only, local) | Success: the owner unlocks the admin web and creates, resets, bans, unbans or, on request, deletes an account (erased and disabled, D-110), sets premium flags (V2), and runs the export, media copy and purge; each action shows its result and writes an audit row (D-102, D-106). / Empty: with no account but the owner's, Accounts lists only the owner; an empty audit log shows "No actions yet". / Offline: every panel that needs Supabase shows "unavailable"; nothing is queued, because the admin web has no offline mode. / Permission-denied: without the passcode only the unlock screen loads, and a request from another origin or host is refused (D-104). The owner's own account cannot be banned or deleted (D-108). / Validation: email syntax per D-116, username and display name per D-89, and a CSV of at most 100 rows, previewed before any account is created (D-81, D-105). |
---TABLE END---

Cross-cutting requirements for all flows
Loading: any wait over 300 ms shows a loading indicator (D-35, D-95).
Error: send failures use the failed status (LS-2); every other error shows its text from the strings module, with Retry where the action can be repeated (D-35, D-95).
Accessibility: platform defaults (D-35, D-54), checked by D-95: a label on every interactive element, text contrast of at least 4.5:1 and, on web, a Tab order that follows the visual order.
Responsive and device behavior: the Expo app targets Android phones; the Next.js app uses a responsive layout (Section 6.1). Breakpoints and tablet behavior: D-35.
Light/dark theming: both themes on both clients (F9).
Security and privacy: Section 5. Push payload privacy: D-25.
Performance: message delivery latency is under 2 seconds at the 95th percentile on a good connection (D-35).
V2 and V3 flows: acceptance tests in Section 7.4 (D-67).
4. Technical Mechanism: Telegram vs DoloGram
| Concern | Telegram | DoloGram |
| Transport | MTProto, a custom protocol for authentication, message framing and encryption. | Supabase client SDK over HTTPS/WSS. No custom protocol; authentication, data access and realtime updates go through one managed backend. |
| Storage architecture | Chat history and media are stored server-side, which enables multi-device sync. | Chat data in Supabase Postgres; media in Supabase Storage; both reachable from the Expo and Next.js apps. Device-only data: unsent queue items (LS-1) and Secret Chat plaintext (D-33). |
| Datacenters | Many distributed data centers. | One Supabase project in one region, by design; the region is chosen when the project is created (D-48: Tokyo). |
| Realtime delivery | Updates pushed over persistent connections. | Supabase Realtime: Postgres Changes deliver row changes (new messages, edits, reactions) from the database replication stream; Broadcast carries typing, read hints (D-92) and, in V3, call signalling and invitations (D-98), all ephemeral (D-14); online status is not built (D-55). |
| Secret Chats | End-to-end encrypted and device-specific. Regular cloud chats are encrypted client-server and stored encrypted; Telegram holds the keys (Telegram E2EE FAQ). | V2: client-side encryption with libsodium, so Supabase stores only ciphertext for that chat type and DoloGram never holds the keys. Key management: D-33. |
| Bot layer | Separate Bot API on top of the core protocol. | None (Section 3.3). Every write to Supabase comes from a human-driven client action. |
---TABLE END---
5. Security & Anti-Bypass Design
Security controls are enforced by the backend, not by the client: auth, encryption and access rules cannot be bypassed by modifying a client.
5.1 Authentication
Supabase Auth with email and password. The owner creates every account; public sign-up is disabled and no email is sent (D-47; phone sign-in stays removed, D-04).
The minimum password length is 12 characters. A forgotten password is reset by the owner with a script that uses the secret key (D-47).
A new or reset account can read and write nothing until it changes its password, and a banned account loses access at once: every policy calls is_active_user(), which reads auth.users rather than the session token (D-78, D-79, D-86). A Realtime channel joined before the change re-checks its authorization only when the client sends a new JWT, so it stops within the 15-minute JWT lifetime (D-114).
The owner's account actions run from the local admin web or the scripts, which share one implementation; both use the secret key on the owner's computer only (D-102, D-104).
Supabase issues JWT session tokens; the Supabase client SDK refreshes them automatically in the Expo and Next.js apps.
Optional app lock on mobile using Expo local authentication, independent of the network session. Phase: D-21.
5.2 Authorization: Row Level Security (RLS)
RLS is enabled on every table in the public schema.
SELECT on chats, messages, message_media, reactions and chat_members returns only rows of chats where the user has a chat_members row. A message_media row of a deleted or expired message is not returned (D-75, D-69).
INSERT on messages succeeds only when sender_id = auth.uid() and the user is a member of the chat.
Message integrity is enforced by triggers: the empty-text rule, the attachment count, same-chat replies, the content length per chat type and the binding of each message_media row to its own uploaded object (D-90).
UPDATE on messages (edit, soft delete) succeeds only for the sender. Further rules: D-22.
Group admin actions (remove member, promote, restrict) are checked by a role check inside the policy, not only hidden in the UI (V2). Roles and restrict: D-71, D-112. Membership rules: D-70, D-124.
Operations that this section does not list (INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE on chats, chat_members, reactions and message_media; every operation on profiles) are decided in D-38 and the policy table in 10.5. An operation without a policy is denied: with RLS enabled, an operation without a policy is denied by default.
Profiles are readable by their owner and by users who share a chat with the caller. To find someone, a user calls find_user with an exact username and gets back one row (user id and display name), limited to 30 calls per minute (D-42, D-46, D-88). premium_flags is readable with the rest of the profile and writable only by the owner (D-87). In the MVP no client may update a profile; editing comes in V2 (D-89).
5.3 Storage Access Control
Chat media lives in two private buckets by size class: chat-media-small (photos and voice notes, 10 MB) and chat-media-large (videos and files, 50 MB), so Storage enforces each size cap and type list (D-75). Each object path is {chat_id}/{media_id} (D-39), so a policy checks membership at upload time, before the message_media row exists. Downloads and signed URLs also require a message_media row for the object whose message is neither deleted nor expired, so a deleted or expired message's files cannot be downloaded and get no new signed URL from that moment; a signed URL issued earlier works until it expires, at most 10 minutes (D-75, D-113).
Storage checks the Content-Type that the client declares. Assumption: Supabase Storage compares allowed_mime_types with the declared type, not the file bytes; accepted, because every reader is a chat member and the apps never execute a file (D-90).
Media downloads use time-limited signed URLs; the buckets are private. Lifetime: 10 minutes (D-113).
5.4 Transport and Secret Chats
All client-server traffic uses HTTPS/WSS endpoints.
Secret Chats (V2): content is encrypted on the client before upload.
5.5 Abuse & Integrity Safeguards
Rate limit on message sends, so a faulty retry loop cannot flood a chat. The limit MUST allow a client to flush a full offline queue. Limit: a token bucket per user with a burst of 200 and a sustained 60 messages per minute, enforced by a database trigger (D-29). User lookup is limited to 30 per minute and chat creation to 20 per day per user in the same way (D-46). Capacities and refill rates: D-122.
Message text is rendered as Markdown; HTML and malformed Markdown are displayed as text and never break the renderer (D-05).
File type and size are validated on upload, on the client and by the Storage bucket limits (D-75), and the message_media trigger checks that the object's declared type matches its media_type (D-90). Size caps: photos and voice notes 10 MB, videos and files 50 MB, with 1 MB = 1,048,576 bytes (D-116). Allowed types: image/jpeg, image/png, image/webp; video/mp4, video/webm; audio/mp4, audio/mpeg, audio/ogg, audio/webm; application/pdf, text/plain, application/zip and Office documents (docx, xlsx, pptx). Every other type is refused (D-29).
5.6 Explicitly Out of Scope
Formal penetration testing, bug bounty and compliance audits (SOC 2, GDPR tooling).
Protection against state-level adversaries; standard RLS, TLS and optional E2EE are the target.
6. React Native Expo, Next.js, Supabase Architecture Plan
6.1 High-Level Architecture
One Supabase backend is consumed by two independent frontends, an Expo app for Android and a Next.js app for the web, so there is one data model and one set of security rules. The owner's local admin web (6.6) uses the same backend with the secret key, on the server side only.
| Layer | Technology | Responsibility |
| Mobile client | React Native (Expo development build; D-11) | Chat UI, camera/media picker, local SQLite queue for Low Signal Mode, push notifications. |
| Web client | Next.js (React) | The same flows as mobile (Section 3.5), browser media upload, responsive layout. Compression and Wi-Fi deferral are mobile-only (D-19). |
| Admin web | Next.js (local only, apps/admin) | The owner's accounts, premium flags, health checks and maintenance jobs; never deployed and no access to message content (Section 6.6, D-102, D-103). |
| Backend | Supabase (Postgres, Auth, Realtime, Storage) | Single source of truth for both clients. |
| Realtime sync | Supabase Realtime | Postgres Changes for message, media and reaction rows; Broadcast for typing and read hints (Presence is not used, D-118; online status is not built, D-55). |
| Media storage | Supabase Storage (two private buckets, D-75) | Photos, videos, voice notes and documents, accessed through signed URLs. |
| Local persistence | Mobile: expo-sqlite (queue, cached history) and AsyncStorage (preferences). Web: IndexedDB | Offline queue and cached chat history (D-06). Device preferences (the Low Signal Mode toggle and the theme) use AsyncStorage on mobile and localStorage on web (D-83, D-100). |
| Push notifications | Expo Notifications and Expo Push service (Android) | New-message alerts while the app is in the background. Android requires FCM credentials and a development build (D-11). Expo's push service has no charge (Expo push FAQ, checked 26 October 2026); FCM credentials come from a Firebase project on the free plan (Assumption: no billing account is needed; checked at M5-10, fallback: push is not built, D-85). Sender mechanism: D-25. |
---TABLE END---

6.2 Data Model
Core tables for the MVP, as decided in D-22 to D-26, D-31, D-32, D-39 and D-40. V2 tables and columns are fixed by D-67 to D-72 and listed at the end of the table, marked V2; the M5 migrations create them.
| Table | Columns | Notes |
| profiles | user_id, username, display_name, avatar_url, premium_flags | A deleted account keeps its row with username deleted_ plus 8 hex digits and display name "Deleted account" (D-110). status is dropped and premium_flags is set only by the owner and readable with the profile (D-32, D-87). username matches ^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{4,31}$ and is unique; display_name is 1 to 64 characters after trimming (D-89). Profiles are readable by their owner and by users who share a chat; other users are found through find_user (D-42). |
| chats | chat_id, type (direct / group), title, created_by, created_at, next_seq, direct_key | type has a CHECK for direct and group (secret is added in V2); title is 1 to 64 characters after trimming for a group and null otherwise, by CHECK (D-109); channel is out of scope and is not a stored value (D-80, D-91). next_seq is the per-chat sequence counter (D-40); clients cannot write it. direct_key holds the sorted member ids of a direct chat (the user id alone for Saved Messages), is unique and is set by a trigger, so one direct chat exists per pair (D-70). |
| chat_members | chat_id, user_id, role (owner / admin / member), joined_at, last_read_seq, archived, muted_until | Roles: D-26. last_read_seq never decreases (D-23). archived and muted_until are per-user settings (D-24); muting is a notification setting only (D-09). |
| messages | message_id, chat_id, sender_id, content, reply_to, attachment_count, seq, sync_seq, edited_at, deleted_at, created_at | message_id is generated on the client (D-10). seq, sync_seq and the three timestamps are assigned by the server (D-40). seq is assigned once, at insert, and orders the chat; sync_seq takes a new value from the same counter on every insert or update and drives resync. attachment_count (0 to 10) is written at insert and never changes; text with no attachment must not be blank, a reply_to must be in the same chat, and content is at most 4096 characters, counted as Unicode code points (D-30, D-90, D-116). |
| message_media | media_id, message_id, chat_id, storage_path, media_type, compressed (bool), sync_seq | compressed is true when LS-5 compression was applied. media_id is generated on the client and storage_path is {chat_id}/{media_id} (D-39). chat_id is copied from the parent message by a trigger. media_type: photo, video, voice or file; photo and voice go to chat-media-small, video and file to chat-media-large (D-75). storage_path is set by the trigger, and the insert requires an object at that path in the bucket of its media_type, uploaded by the caller, with a matching declared type (D-90). |
| reactions | message_id, chat_id, user_id, emoji, removed_at, sync_seq | Primary key (message_id, user_id, emoji). Removal sets removed_at; no DELETE is granted (D-22). Re-adding clears removed_at under the same limit, and emoji has a CHECK for the palette (D-76); from V2 the CHECK lists 12 emoji and the trigger gates the 4 extra ones (D-119). chat_id is copied from the parent message. Palette of 8 emoji; at most 3 active reactions per user per message. |
| folders | folder_id, user_id, name | Per-user folders: name 1 to 32 characters (CHECK), at most 10 folders per user (trigger) (D-24, D-94). |
| folder_chats | folder_id, chat_id | A chat can be in several folders. Deleting a folder deletes its folder_chats rows (ON DELETE CASCADE), never the chats (D-94). |
| rate_limits (internal) | user_id, action, tokens, refilled_at | Token buckets keyed by user and action for sends, user lookup and chat creation, added in M3-08 (D-46). No client access. |
| private.disabled_users (internal) | user_id, created_at | Fallback ban list read by is_active_user() (D-86). No client access. |
| private.initial_passwords (internal) | user_id, password_hash | Used only if the D-78 trigger is refused (D-86); the hash is never shown. No client access. |
| private.admin_audit (internal) | id, at, action, target_user_id, result, detail | One row per admin web or script action; never holds a password or message content (D-106). No client access. |
| messages (V2 columns) | kind, payload, ttl_seconds, expires_at | kind is text, sticker, location or poll (default text); payload holds sticker_id, or lat, lng and accuracy_m, and is checked per kind by a trigger with the ranges of D-120 (D-67). Only private.expire_messages() may update a message after expires_at (D-111). ttl_seconds is 60, 3600, 86400 or 604800, and the server sets expires_at = created_at + ttl_seconds (D-69). In a secret chat, content holds base64 ciphertext of at most 22,000 characters (D-68), checked by the length trigger of D-90. A message whose kind is not text may have empty content (D-90). sticker_id matches ^[a-z0-9_]{1,64}$ and ids starting with p_ are premium (D-97). |
| chats, chat_members (V2 columns) | chats.type adds secret; chat_members.restricted_until | A secret chat is 1-on-1, mobile-only and text-only; its second member must already share a direct chat with the first, and direct_key stays null (D-33, D-68, D-91). A restricted member cannot send, edit, react or vote until restricted_until, and may still delete their own messages and remove their own reactions and votes (D-71, D-112). |
| polls (V2) | poll_id (= message_id), chat_id, question, options, closed_at, sync_seq | Question at most 300 characters; 2 to 10 options of at most 100 characters; single-choice and non-anonymous; the sender closes the poll by setting closed_at (D-59, D-67). Created only with its message through send_poll; a constraint trigger rejects a poll message without a polls row at commit (D-120). In the supabase_realtime publication. |
| poll_votes (V2) | poll_id, chat_id, user_id, option_index, removed_at, sync_seq | Primary key (poll_id, user_id). A vote changes by UPDATE of option_index and is withdrawn by removed_at, never by DELETE; votes after closed_at are rejected (D-67); option_index is 0 to the number of options minus 1 (D-120). In the supabase_realtime publication. |
| secret_keys (V2) | chat_id, user_id, public_key, created_at | Primary key (chat_id, user_id). One X25519 public key per member, inserted once from the device; no UPDATE, so a new key means a new chat (D-49, D-68). |
| push_tokens (V2) | token, user_id, created_at, last_seen_at | Own rows only. The Edge Function deletes a token that the Expo Push API reports as invalid (D-25, D-67). |
| profiles (V2 values) | premium_flags (text array), avatar_url | premium_flags holds badge, extra_reactions or premium_stickers and is set only by the owner. avatar_url is a path {user_id}/{uuid} in the private avatars bucket, at most 512 KB; empty in the MVP (D-72). |
| Local queue record (device only: expo-sqlite on mobile, IndexedDB on web) | temp_id, payload, status, retry_count, next_attempt_at | temp_id equals the message_id the item will use (D-10). status values: LS-2. Every item not yet sent counts toward the 200-item limit (D-93). |
| Local sync state (device only) | chat_id, cursor | cursor is the highest sync_seq received for that chat (D-40). |
---TABLE END---

6.3 Low Signal Mode Implementation Notes
Mobile network detection uses @react-native-community/netinfo: connection type (wifi or cellular) and, for cellular, the generation (2g to 5g, or null when it cannot be determined). It does not report cellular signal strength (D-13). Assumption: the library version matches the Expo SDK in use; checked at M0-08 and M3-05 (D-85 item 14), fallback: only the Forced row applies.
The queue worker runs on connectivity-change events and on a timer while the app is in the foreground (LS-3, D-117). Attempt timing and counting: D-77.
Compression uses expo-image-manipulator on photos only. Videos are not transcoded; they are deferred per LS-5 (D-07). Profile: D-28, D-117.
The queue is a local expo-sqlite table with the status values in LS-2, shown in the chat UI.
Web: IndexedDB queue; retry on the browser online event and on a timer. The Network Information API and Background Sync are not relied on because browser support is limited (D-12).
6.4 Realtime & Sync Flow
A client inserts a messages row, queued locally first (LS-1). Supabase Realtime Postgres Changes delivers the change to every subscribed member's client.
messages, message_media and reactions are added to the supabase_realtime publication, and polls and poll_votes join it in V2 (D-67). Postgres Changes delivers INSERT and UPDATE events only for rows the subscriber may SELECT. DELETE events are not RLS-filtered, so messages are soft-deleted by UPDATE (D-14). A reaction DELETE would also reach every subscriber without RLS filtering (Supabase Postgres Changes documentation), so no DELETE is granted and a reaction is removed by UPDATE of removed_at (D-22).
Typing and read hints use the per-chat Broadcast channel chat:<chat_id> (D-92); Presence is refused (D-118); in V3, call signalling uses call:<chat_id> and each foreground client joins user:<user_id> for call invitations (D-98). Online status is not built (D-55). The channels are private, authorized by RLS policies on realtime.messages, and "Allow public access" is disabled in the Supabase Realtime settings so that public channels are refused (Supabase Realtime Authorization documentation). Realtime caches a client's authorization per connection and re-checks it on each channel join and each new JWT (same documentation, checked 31 October 2026), so the 15-minute JWT expiry bounds how long a revoked user stays in a joined channel (D-114).
Both clients subscribe to the same channels.
On app foreground or reconnect, the client re-subscribes and, for each chat it holds, fetches the rows of messages, message_media and reactions whose sync_seq is greater than that chat's stored cursor, in pages, until none remain (D-40). The cursor is the highest sync_seq received for the chat; the device clock is never used.
A chat that no longer appears in the chat list fetch, because the user left or was removed, has its local cache and sync state deleted on that device (D-91).
The server assigns seq and sync_seq from the per-chat counter chats.next_seq. The counter row stays locked until the writing transaction commits, so sequence numbers become visible in commit order and no row can appear behind a cursor; the concurrency check of M1-03 and T-13 verify this. Events and fetched rows are merged by key, so a duplicate is harmless (D-40).
Delivered messages are ordered by seq. A message still in the local queue is shown after the last delivered message with its LS-2 status until the server assigns its seq. Read state is chat_members.last_read_seq, set to the seq of the newest message displayed (D-23). The client then sends a read hint on chat:<chat_id>, and members with the chat open re-read chat_members through RLS, so a forged hint changes nothing (D-92).
Realtime limits: each client opens one connection and joins chat:<chat_id> only for the chat that is open, plus user:<user_id> in V3 (D-98), so the 100-channels-per-connection cap is never reached. Messages reach the app through a small interface in packages/shared. Postgres Changes serves the Free plan limit of 200 concurrent clients in this design; when the monthly peak reaches 160 connections (80%), database Broadcast replaces it behind the same interface, and resync by sync_seq keeps the data correct either way (D-43, D-123). Assumption: Supabase recommends Broadcast over Postgres Changes at larger scale; checked at M1-04 (D-85 item 15); the switch rule of D-123 holds either way.
6.5 Deployment (Personal Use Only)
Expo: the Android app is built only with EAS Build on the Free plan, in the cloud (eas build --platform android), which produces an APK that is installed directly on the device. Expo Go is not sufficient (D-11). No Play Store submission, no store account, no Android Studio and no local build (D-66). EAS Free allows 15 Android builds a month (Expo pricing page, checked 26 October 2026). With a development build, JavaScript changes reload from the dev server and need no new build, so the quota is ample.
Next.js: Vercel Hobby plan, production at the free *.vercel.app address, deployed from the main branch; next dev on the owner's computer is for development only (D-115).
Supabase: Free plan project in the Tokyo (ap-northeast-1) region (D-48; limits in Section 7.1).
Auth email: none. Sign-in uses email and password, so Supabase sends no email and no SMTP provider is needed (D-47).
Implementation: Section 10.8 gives the release order and Section 10.2 the environment this deployment needs.
6.6 Admin Web
The admin web is the owner's control panel for accounts and operations (D-102). It runs only on the owner's computer at http://127.0.0.1:3100, started with npm run admin, and is never deployed. It replaces running the account and maintenance scripts by hand; the scripts stay, and both call packages/admin-core.
No content: the admin web has no function that reads messages, media, reactions, polls, chat titles, secret keys or per-user activity, and it cannot sign in as a user (D-103).
Access: a passcode checked against ADMIN_PASSCODE_HASH, a SameSite=Strict session cookie with an 8-hour idle timeout, a 5-minute lockout after 5 wrong passcodes, and Origin and Host checks on every mutation (D-104).
Secrets: SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY, SUPABASE_DB_URL and SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN are read only by server actions and route handlers (D-102).
Passwords: first and reset passwords are 16 characters generated on the server, shown once and never stored or logged (D-105).
Audit: every action writes a row to private.admin_audit (D-106).
Management API: project status, usage and settings come from the Supabase Management API; a panel that cannot be read shows "unavailable" with a Dashboard link (D-107).
| Screen | Purpose | Functions |
| ADM-01 Unlock | Keep other programs and browser tabs out. | Passcode; 5 wrong attempts lock the screen for 5 minutes; sets the session cookie (D-104). |
| ADM-02 Overview | One look at the state of the service. | Project status with Restore when paused; a bar per 7.1 limit, amber at 80% and red at 100% (D-45), with the action of D-123; account totals (active, banned, must change password); last export, media copy and purge, each marked due or overdue; settings summary from ADM-08 (D-107). |
| ADM-03 Accounts | Find an account. | Table of username, display name, email, created, last sign-in, status and premium flags (V2); search by username or email; filters All, Active, Banned, Must change password; 50 per page. |
| ADM-04 Create account | Add contacts. | Single form (email, username, display name) or CSV import of at most 100 rows with a preview; each account gets a generated first password and must_change_password (D-47, D-78, D-81); passwords are shown once, with Copy or Download as CSV (D-105). |
| ADM-05 Account detail | Act on one account. | Reset password (new password first, flag second, D-78); Ban and Unban (D-61, D-86); premium switches badge, extra_reactions and premium_stickers (V2, D-72); Delete account, only on request and after typing the username; it erases and disables the account (D-108, D-110). |
| ADM-06 Maintenance | Run the runbook jobs. | Weekly database export with supabase db dump into BACKUP_DIR (D-29); monthly media copy of every bucket (D-74); monthly purge with a preview of counts and sizes, never contents (D-75); restore-drill checklist. One job runs at a time. |
| ADM-07 Audit log | See what was done. | Time, action, target username, result and counts, newest first, filtered by action or target; no passwords or content (D-106). |
| ADM-08 Settings check | Confirm the project still matches 10.2. | Pass or Fail for sign-up off, phone off, minimum password length 12, JWT expiry 900 seconds (D-114), Realtime public access off, the publication tables, RLS on every public table and the region; "Check by hand" with a Dashboard link when the API cannot read a setting (D-107). |
---TABLE END---
Implementation: M4-08 builds the admin web; T-42 to T-48 and T-53 verify it. Section 11.4 describes each screen in full.
7. Risk Mitigation & Test Matrix
7.1 Key Risks & Mitigations
| Risk | Severity | Mitigation |
| Supabase Free plan limits exceeded | Medium | Limits (Supabase pricing page): 500 MB database, 1 GB file storage, 5 GB egress plus 5 GB cached egress, 50 MB max upload per file, 200 concurrent Realtime connections, 2 million Realtime messages per month (Supabase billing documentation, checked 31 October 2026, D-125). Low Signal compression reduces media size. The supported scale is 50,000 monthly active users, 200 concurrent Realtime connections and 100 Realtime messages per second (D-41); the owner checks the Supabase usage dashboard monthly and acts at 80% of any limit with the actions of D-123 (D-45). |
| Free project paused after 1 week of inactivity | Medium | Supabase pauses Free projects with low database activity over 7 days, after a warning email a week before, and a paused project can be restored for 90 days (Supabase project pausing documentation, checked 31 October 2026, D-125); the owner unpauses it from the admin web or the dashboard. |
| No automatic backups on the Free plan | Medium | The Free plan does not include automatic backups (Supabase pricing page). Backup: a weekly manual database export (D-29). |
| RLS misconfiguration exposes private chats | High | Every RLS policy and Realtime authorization rule in Sections 5.2, 5.3 and 6.4 has a test (T-05, T-06, T-11, T-14, T-15, T-16, T-22, T-38, T-39, T-51, T-52), run before a new feature ships. Client-side filtering is never the control. |
| Offline queue creates duplicate sends on retry | Medium | LS-8: message_id is the idempotency key; verified by T-09. |
| Poor UX when signal is lost mid-send | Medium | LS-2: statuses are always visible and nothing fails silently; verified by T-18 and T-41. |
| Realtime channel drops on app background/foreground (mobile) | Medium | Re-subscribe on foreground and fetch missed changes (Section 6.4); verified by T-13. |
| Large video/file uploads fail on 2G/3G | High | LS-5 defers items above the large-media threshold until Wi-Fi and shows "waiting for Wi-Fi". |
| Secret Chat encryption implemented incorrectly | High | Use libsodium, not custom cryptography. react-native-libsodium implements only a subset of the libsodium-wrappers API (react-native-libsodium README), so confirm that every required function is available. Test with known plaintext/ciphertext vectors. |
| Lost or leaked password | Medium | Minimum 12 characters, Supabase sign-in rate limits and an owner-run reset with the secret key (D-47); verified by T-10. |
| Inconsistent behavior between Expo and Next.js clients | Low | Both clients implement the same requirements in this document; shared validation rules are in Section 5.5. |
| Admin web exposes the secret key or private data | High | Local-only on 127.0.0.1, passcode, Origin and Host checks, the secret key in server code only, and no function that reads content (D-102 to D-104); verified by T-42 to T-44. |
---TABLE END---

Implementation: Section 10.9 maps each risk to plan tasks.
7.2 Core Test Matrix
| ID | Scenario | Pass criteria | Verifies |
| T-01 | Send text with a good connection | The recipient's client shows the message without a manual refresh. Budget: under 2 s at the 95th percentile over 100 messages (D-35), with both clients reading one clock (D-82). | F3 |
| T-02 | Send text with no connection, then reconnect | The message shows queued while offline, is delivered after reconnection, and is stored exactly once. | LS-1, LS-3, LS-8 |
| T-03 | Send a photo on a simulated 2G/3G connection | The photo is compressed (message_media.compressed is true) and shows "sending (low signal)". The test injects the network state; the physical device is the only manual check (D-65). | LS-2, LS-5 |
| T-04 | Same account on two devices | Message history and read status are identical on both. | F2, D-23 |
| T-05 | Non-member reads a chat through a direct API call | The query returns no rows for that chat's chats row, chat_members rows, messages, media and reactions. | Section 5.2 |
| T-06 | Edit and delete a message | The sender's edit or delete shows on all members' screens in real time. The same update by another member is rejected. | F4 |
| T-07 | Kill the app mid-upload and reopen | The queued item is retried after restart and delivered once; it is not lost. | LS-9 |
| T-08 | Turn on Force Low Signal Mode | On a 4G or 5G cellular connection (the Normal row), new text is sent immediately. New photos are compressed and show "waiting for Wi-Fi", and so do new videos, voice notes and files, until Wi-Fi is connected. A photo queued offline before the toggle was turned on is sent on reconnection without Low Signal compression (LS-6, D-117). | LS-5, LS-6, D-117 |
| T-09 | Drop the connection after the server stored a message but before the client received the reply | After the retry, the chat contains exactly one copy of the message. | LS-8 |
| T-10 | Sign in with email and password | A correct email and password create a session; a wrong password does not, and repeated wrong attempts are rate-limited. A password shorter than 12 characters is refused. Sign-up for an unknown address is refused, no phone-number sign-in option exists and no email is sent. Changing the password in settings works while signed in. A new account is sent to Change password at first sign-in. Until it changes the password, an API read with its session returns no rows (D-78). | F1, D-47 |
| T-11 | Non-member requests a media file | Upload and download are denied. A signed URL stops working after its 10-minute lifetime (D-113). | Section 5.3, D-113 |
| T-12 | Send text containing HTML tags and malformed Markdown | Both clients display the text literally and keep rendering; a heading renders as plain text and an image link loads no image (D-99). This settles the web assumption in Section 3.2. | Section 5.5 |
| T-13 | Background the app, receive messages and edits from another device, then foreground | The app re-subscribes and shows the missed messages, edits, media rows and reactions, fetched by sync_seq greater than its stored cursor. Messages written by two senders at the same moment all arrive and none is skipped. | Section 6.4 |
| T-14 | Non-member subscribes to a chat's typing channel | The subscription is refused or delivers no events. A Presence join of the channel by a member is refused (D-118). | Section 6.4, D-118 |
| T-15 | Insert a message as another user, and insert a message into a chat the user is not a member of, through a direct API call | Both inserts are rejected and no row is created. | F3, Section 5.2 |
| T-16 | List every table in the public schema with its row-level-security flag (catalog query) | Every table shows RLS enabled. A catalog query may run in the SQL Editor because it reads metadata and does not test a policy. | Section 5.2 |
| T-17 | Reply to a message, forward a message, add a reaction, then remove it | The reply stores reply_to. The forwarded message appears in the target chat with the copied content. The reaction and its removal are visible to all members without a manual refresh. The removal is an UPDATE of removed_at; a direct DELETE through the API is denied and removes no row. In the MVP, forwarding a photo message forwards its text only, shows the notice and copies no object (D-50). | F3, D-50 |
| T-18 | Make every delivery attempt fail until the retries are exhausted | The item shows failed and stays visible; nothing is lost silently. Retry parameters: D-27, D-77. | LS-7 |
| T-19 | Web client: send text while offline, then go online | The message is queued in IndexedDB, delivered after the browser reports it is online, and stored exactly once. | LS-10 |
| T-20 | Send a photo with text on a throttled connection | The text reaches other members before the upload completes. The item shows sent only after every attachment is uploaded. | LS-4, F5 |
| T-21 | Send a burst of messages through the API as one user | A burst of 200 messages is accepted. Further messages beyond the refill rate of 60 per minute are rejected and create no row. A client queue of 200 items then drains with no item lost once tokens refill; rate-limited items wait without using attempts (D-77). The 31st user lookup within a minute and the 21st chat created within a day are rejected the same way, with the bucket parameters of D-122 (D-46). | Section 5.5, LS-7, D-29, D-122 |
| T-22 | Read profiles and look up users through the API | User C, who shares no chat with A, reads zero rows of A's profile. find_user with A's exact username in any letter case returns one row holding only user id and display name (D-88); a partial username returns nothing. | Section 5.2, D-42, D-88 |
| T-36 | Archive, mute and file chats in folders on one device, then open a second device | The main list shows chats that are not archived, newest message first, then chats with no messages by creation time; archived chats appear only in Archive and stay there when a message arrives; a muted chat keeps its place with an indicator; a folder lists its chats that are not archived; deleting a folder leaves its chats; the second device shows every change after foreground; offline the controls are disabled. Through the API an eleventh folder and a 33-character folder name are rejected, and C can neither read nor write A's folders. | F2, F9, D-24, D-94 |
| T-37 | B reads A's message while A has the chat open | A's client shows the message as seen within 2 seconds without a refresh; in the A+B group it shows seen once one other member has read it. A non-member's read hint is refused, and a member's forged hint does not change what A sees. The unread count leaves out deleted messages. | F6, D-23, D-92 |
| T-38 | Membership changes through the API | A second direct chat for the same pair and a third member in a direct chat are rejected; adding a user who shares no chat with the adder is rejected; a member who leaves a group reads nothing more from it; when the owner leaves, the earliest-joined member becomes owner; a chat with type channel is rejected; a removed member's client deletes the chat from its cache on the next foreground; a 101st member is rejected; the creator's first owner row of a new group is accepted and an owner row inserted by anyone else is rejected (D-124). | 3.2 Chat, D-58, D-70, D-91, D-124 |
| T-39 | Insert messages and media rows that break the integrity rules, through the API | Each is rejected and creates no row: blank text with attachment_count 0, a second media row on a message with attachment_count 1, attachment_count 11, a reply_to naming a message of another chat, a media row for an object another user uploaded, a photo row for an audio object, 4097 characters of text, and a client-written edited_at. Blank text with attachment_count 1 is accepted. A text of 4096 emoji (4096 code points, 8192 UTF-16 units) is accepted and one of 4097 is rejected; text of only spaces, tabs and line feeds counts as blank (D-116). | Section 5.5, D-30, D-76, D-90, D-116 |
| T-40 | Theme, loading, errors and accessibility on both clients | System, Light and Dark apply to every screen and the choice survives a restart; a wait over 300 ms shows a loading indicator; a forced error shows its text and, where the action can be repeated, Retry; the label checks find no unlabeled interactive element; the contrast script reports at least 4.5:1 for text in both themes; on web, Tab follows the visual order; at 767 px the web layout is one column and at 768 px two. | 3.2 Dark mode, Section 3.5, D-35, D-83, D-95 |
| T-41 | Queue order and failed-item actions | With the toggle on and a cellular connection, a text queued after a video in the same chat is delivered at once, the video's message keeps the lower seq, and B sees a placeholder until Wi-Fi connects. Retry resets a failed item's count and delivers it. Discard of a failed item whose row exists is disabled offline and, online, soft-deletes the row, which B sees as the placeholder; Discard of an item the server never stored creates no row. A 201st unsent item is refused. | LS-4, LS-5, LS-7, D-93 |
| T-42 | Scan the web build, the mobile bundle and the admin client bundle for the secret key, the database URL and the access token | None of the three values is found in any bundle. | Section 5, Section 6.6, D-102 |
| T-43 | Search the admin source for reads of content, and list every server action | No read of messages, message_media, reactions, polls, poll_votes, secret_keys or chat media objects exists, and every server action is one of the functions in Section 6.6. | Section 6.6, D-103 |
| T-44 | Reach the admin web without unlocking, from another origin and with a wrong Host header | Each request is refused and changes nothing; 5 wrong passcodes lock the screen for 5 minutes. | F12, D-104 |
| T-45 | Create an account in the admin web and sign in with it on W | Sign-in leads to Change password, and until the password changes an API read returns no rows (D-78); the generated password appears neither in private.admin_audit nor in BACKUP_DIR. | F12, D-47, D-78, D-105 |
| T-46 | Import a CSV of three valid rows and one invalid row | The preview marks the invalid row; Create makes exactly three accounts, each with a Saved Messages chat. | F12, D-81, D-105 |
| T-47 | Reset a password, then ban and unban the account | After the reset the old password fails and the account is sent to Change password; after the ban the next API read with a live session is denied (D-86) and a new join of chat:<chat_id> is refused, and a channel joined before the ban stops within 15 minutes (D-114); after the unban it works again; each step has an audit row. | F12, D-61, D-78, D-86, D-106, D-114 |
| T-48 | Run the export, the media copy twice, the purge preview and the purge | The export file exists; the second copy run copies nothing new; the preview counts match what the purge deletes; the Overview shows each job as done; the export restores into a scratch local stack with matching row counts; a usage figure entered by hand at 80% of a limit shows that bar amber (D-123). | F12, D-29, D-74, D-75, D-106, D-123 |
| T-49 | Apply the Section 11 UI rules on D and W | Each mute option sets muted_until to the matching time, and Until I unmute sets infinity; a 65-character group title is rejected through the API; Forward is hidden on a message with no text; Sign out with two unsent items warns, then leaves the local queue and cache empty; a queued item does not send while the app is in the background and sends on reopening; on W, Enter sends, Shift+Enter adds a line and a pasted image is attached; date separators read Today and Yesterday. | Section 11, D-109 |
| T-50 | Run the web smoke set in each supported browser | In current Chrome, Edge, Firefox and Safari on desktop and Chrome on Android: sign-in, sending and receiving text, sending a photo and a voice note, the offline queue of T-19 and the theme switch all work. | Section 11.3, LS-10, D-109 |
| T-51 | A types in the A-B chat while B watches on the other client; then A and G type at once in the A+B+G group | B's client shows "A is typing..." within 2 seconds; while A types for 10 seconds the channel carries at most 4 typing events (D-57); the indicator hides 5 seconds after A's last event, and at once when A's message arrives; in the group B sees "2 people are typing..." (D-118). | F6, 3.2 Typing, D-57, D-118 |
| T-52 | Send each media type, then test the bucket limits through the API | A sends a photo, a video, a voice note and a file, and B opens each on the other client. As A through the API, a photo object of exactly 10,485,760 bytes is accepted by chat-media-small and one of 10,485,761 bytes is refused; a file of 52,428,801 bytes is refused by chat-media-large; an object declared as application/x-msdownload is refused by both buckets (D-29, D-75, D-116). | F5, 3.2 Photos, videos, voice notes, files, Section 5.5, D-29, D-75, D-116 |
| T-53 | Delete an account on request in the admin web | A throwaway account that sent a message and a reaction in a group with A and a message in a direct chat with A is deleted. On A's client, after foreground and without a manual refresh, its messages show "Deleted message", its reaction is gone and it is shown as "Deleted account"; an API read as A returns those messages with empty content and deleted_at set; no row was hard-deleted from messages, message_media or reactions; sign-in with its old email fails; private.admin_audit holds one row with the counts; Delete is disabled on the owner's account (D-108). | F12, D-108, D-110 |

Implementation: Section 10.7 gives the milestone where each test first runs, its setup and its environments.
7.3 Suggested Testing Approach
Test on at least two physical devices, or one device plus the web client, to validate Realtime sync. Use test accounts only.
Simulate low signal with Android network throttling and airplane-mode toggling.
Test RLS by calling the API with each test user's own session. The Supabase SQL Editor runs as a role that bypasses RLS by default, so results there do not prove a policy works (D-16).
7.4 V2 and V3 Acceptance Tests
These tests define V2 and V3 completion (D-67); a test made not applicable by a D-85 fallback counts as passed when the test log records the fallback (D-121). Like 7.2 they run through the API with each test user's own session wherever a policy is involved (D-16), and every new table also gets the RLS checks of T-05, T-15 and T-16. T-23 to T-33 gate V2 (M5-11) and T-34 and T-35 gate V3.
| ID | Scenario | Pass criteria | Verifies |
| T-23 | Android build in the background; another member sends a message | A notification shows the chat name and "New message"; the payload carries only chat_id. A muted chat raises none. With the OS permission denied no alert appears and the app otherwise works. A webhook call without the PUSH_WEBHOOK_SECRET bearer token gets 401 and sends nothing; neither the sender nor a banned member is notified; a direct-chat notification is titled with the sender's display name (D-96). If Firebase demands a billing account, push is not built and this test is recorded as not applicable (D-85), which counts as passed for the V2 gate (D-121). | F11, D-25, D-49, D-96 |
| T-24 | Turn on app lock, background the app past the re-lock time, reopen | The chat list stays hidden until local authentication succeeds; a failure keeps it locked; it works offline; the Immediately, 1 minute and 5 minutes options apply. A device with no enrolled credential cannot turn the lock on. | F10, D-21, D-49 |
| T-25 | Send a sticker and a GIF | The recipient renders both from the bundle; a network inspector shows no request to a GIF or sticker service; both messages have kind sticker (D-120). A user without premium_stickers cannot send a premium sticker (an id starting with p_): the API insert is rejected (D-97). | 3.2 Stickers & GIFs, D-59, D-72, D-97, D-120 |
| T-26 | Create a poll, vote, change the vote, close it | Members see the counts update without a refresh. A poll with 1 or 11 options is rejected; a poll message inserted without its polls row is rejected at commit, and a vote with an option_index outside the options is rejected (D-120); a vote after closing is rejected; a non-member reads no rows and cannot vote through the API; a direct DELETE of a vote is denied. | 3.2 Polls, D-59, D-67, D-120 |
| T-27 | Share the current location; then deny the location permission and try again | The message is a card with the coordinates and an "Open in Maps" link, and no map SDK request is made. | T-28 | Two members on two Android devices start a Secret Chat and exchange messages | Both devices show the same key fingerprint; an API read as a member returns ciphertext only; an API insert of non-base64 content into the secret chat, a third member and a member who shares no direct chat are rejected (D-90, D-91); the libsodium known-answer vectors pass; attachments, reactions and forwarding are unavailable; the web client does not list the chat; a 4096-character message is accepted and a 4097-character one is refused on the client. | 3.2 Secret Chats, D-33, D-68, D-90, D-91 |
| T-29 | Send a message with a photo and a 1-minute timer | At expires_at both clients hide the message, and a download or signed URL for the photo is denied. A signed URL created before expiry stops working within 10 minutes (D-113). | 3.2 Self-destruct, D-59, D-69, D-111, D-113 |
| T-30 | Admin actions through the API by a member, an admin and the owner | A member's remove, promote or restrict is rejected. An admin removes and restricts a member but not the owner or another admin; only the owner promotes and demotes. | T-31 | Premium flags | A user cannot change their own premium_flags through the API; the badge shows for a flagged user, and another member reads premium_flags through the API (D-87); an extra reaction emoji is rejected for a user without extra_reactions and accepted with it; after the flag is removed an active extra reaction stays, and once removed it cannot be re-added (D-119). | 3.2 Premium, D-32, D-72, D-87, D-119 |
| T-32 | Forward a message with a photo to another chat, then delete the original | The target chat holds a new object under its own chat_id and a new message_media row; the photo stays readable in the target chat after the original is deleted; a non-member of the target chat cannot read it. | 3.2 Forward, D-50 |
| T-33 | Edit the profile and the avatar | The owner of the profile changes the display name and avatar; another user's update is denied; a username that breaks the D-89 format or a blank display name is refused; an avatar above 512 KB or of another type is refused; a user who shares no chat cannot read the avatar. | D-51, D-72, D-89 |
| T-34 | 1-on-1 voice call between D and W, on one network and across networks | The call connects on one network. Across networks it connects where STUN succeeds and otherwise ends after 20 seconds with "Call could not connect on this network". A non-member cannot join call:<chat_id>. The callee's open app rings through user:<user_id> even when the chat is closed; an unanswered call ends after 30 seconds with "No answer"; a user who shares no direct chat cannot send to another user's user:<user_id> channel (D-98). 8. Open Decisions
Questions that need the product owner. Each lists the options, a recommended default, and its impact on scope or schedule (the requirements it blocks). IDs are never reused; a closed decision moves to Section 9. There are no open decisions: OD-01 to OD-21 were closed in v4, OD-22 and OD-23 in v5 as D-47 to D-49, and the residual items of v5 in v6 as D-56 to D-65. The deferred items, gaps and ambiguities of v7 are closed in v8 as D-67 to D-84, what v8 left conditional, the remaining gaps and the remaining ambiguities are closed in v9 as D-85 to D-101, and the conditional item, gaps and ambiguities of v11 are closed in v12 as D-110 to D-126.
Implementation: Section 10.1 records what the plan applies and what changes if a closed decision is reversed.
No decision is open. Version 8 also closes what v7 left deferred: the V2 and V3 specifications (D-67 to D-73), the media backup method (D-74), and the gaps and ambiguities found in review (D-75 to D-84). Items that depend on a check keep a named fallback: Firebase billing (D-65: push is skipped), react-native-webrtc on the Expo SDK (D-73: calls are web-only) and the library spikes (D-65). Version 9 lists every remaining check with its check point and fallback in D-85, so no item waits on a later decision, and PF-23 is closed. Version 10 adds the owner's admin web (D-102 to D-108); its Management API checks follow the rules of D-85. Version 11 adds the screen breakdown of Section 11 and its UI rules (D-109). Version 12 closes the conditional account deletion of D-108 (D-110), the gaps and ambiguities of v11 (D-111 to D-124, D-126) and records the claims checked in the Supabase documentation (D-125). The Supabase project already exists (D-56). The owner's remaining actions are tasks: create the accounts (M1-08), supply sticker files (V2) and, if push is wanted, a Firebase project (V2).
