9. Decision Log
| ID | Decision | Source |
| D-01 | The product name is DoloGram everywhere; this document is DoloGram_Documentation_v2 (superseded by D-20). | Product owner request. |
| D-02 | Section 3.2 is the authoritative scope list; Section 2 is a Telegram reference catalog. Drafts are not part of local persistence (Section 6.1) because they have no row in Section 3.2 (D-21). | Prior draft: the scope section stated that it defines what is actually implemented. |
| D-03 | The feature is named Low Signal Mode everywhere to avoid confusion with free-tier hosting. | Naming consistency across the prior draft's feature list, scope table and implementation notes. |
| D-04 | Sign-in was email OTP only; phone OTP is removed. Email OTP is superseded by D-47 (email and password); phone sign-in stays removed. | Supabase Auth docs: phone sign-in needs a third-party SMS provider (Twilio, MessageBird, Vonage, TextLocal), which conflicts with Goal 1.1. Assumption: SMS providers charge per message; not checked, because phone sign-in stays removed whatever the price (D-47). |
| D-05 | Message text supports Markdown only; HTML is displayed as literal text. | Prior draft mapped formatting to a Markdown renderer only; react-markdown documentation: HTML is escaped or ignored by default (which one is unconfirmed; see Section 3.2). |
| D-06 | The offline queue uses expo-sqlite on mobile and IndexedDB on web; AsyncStorage is not used for the queue. | Prior draft: technical mechanism and implementation notes both specified SQLite on Expo and IndexedDB on web. |
| D-07 | Videos are not transcoded; under Low Signal Mode they are deferred until Wi-Fi. | Prior draft named only expo-image-manipulator (photos) and deferred large media until Wi-Fi. |
| D-08 | The membership table is chat_members everywhere (not group_members). | Prior draft data model and RLS list (chat_members). |
| D-09 | The admin action is called "restrict"; "mute" means only a user's notification setting. | One name per concept: admin "mute" and user notification "mute" were both used in the prior draft. |
| D-10 | message_id is generated on the client and doubles as the idempotency key (the queue's temp_id). | Prior draft required de-duplication by temp_id; the messages table has no other column to carry it. |
| D-11 | The Expo app runs as a development build; Expo Go is not used. | Expo docs: remote push on Android is not supported in Expo Go from SDK 53. react-native-libsodium README: native module used with the Expo dev client. |
| D-12 | The web client retries on the browser online event and a timer; it does not rely on Background Sync or the Network Information API. | MDN: Background Sync is unsupported in Firefox and Safari; @react-native-community/netinfo README: browsers report network type as unknown for many networks. |
| D-13 | Network detection uses @react-native-community/netinfo; the automatic trigger is cellular generation 2G or 3G; signal strength is not used. | @react-native-community/netinfo README: cellularGeneration on Android and iOS; strength reported for Wi-Fi only. |
| D-14 | Row changes use Postgres Changes; typing and online status use Broadcast/Presence; messages are soft-deleted by UPDATE. Online status is not built (D-55); Broadcast also carries read hints (D-92) and V3 call signalling and invitations (D-98). Amended by D-118: Presence is not used. | Supabase Realtime docs: Presence and Broadcast are ephemeral; DELETE events are not RLS-filtered. |
| D-15 | Excluded: bots (project scope); public channel discovery (no public directory needed for personal use); live streaming and screen sharing (high infrastructure cost, near-zero personal benefit); faster downloads (needs paid CDN/edge infrastructure); unlimited voice-to-text (needs a paid speech-to-text API). | Scope decisions recorded in the prior draft (non-goals, exclusions, removed paid features). |
| D-16 | RLS is tested through the API with each test user's session, not in the SQL Editor. | Supabase troubleshooting docs: the SQL Editor runs as postgres, which bypasses RLS. |
| D-17 | "sending (low signal)" and "waiting for Wi-Fi" are labels on sending and queued, not additional states. | Prior draft defined four states but its risk and test tables used the two labels. |
| D-18 | After reconnecting, clients fetch messages whose created_at, edited_at or deleted_at is after the last sync time. Superseded by D-40: the cursor is the per-chat sync_seq. | Message columns in Section 6.2; a created_at-only fetch would miss edits and deletes made while offline. |
| D-19 | The web client queues and retries only; photo compression and Wi-Fi deferral are mobile-only. | Closed from OD-09: LS-10 and Section 6.1 already state it, and D-12 shows browsers cannot reliably report Wi-Fi versus cellular. |
| D-20 | This document is DoloGram_Documentation_v14. D-01's reference to v2 is superseded. OD-01 to OD-21 are closed as D-21 to D-40, and OD-22 and OD-23 as D-47 to D-49. | Product owner request for the v4 to v14 revisions; settles PF-24. |
| D-21 | Catalog features that have no Section 3.2 row are out of scope for v1 (the full list is D-80). chats.type = channel is reserved and has no behavior (amended by D-91: channel is not a stored value). App lock is V2; its re-lock timing is D-49. | Closes OD-01 (default A). |
| D-22 | Edit and delete are sender-only with no time limit. Delete is for everyone, clears the stored content and leaves a placeholder; a reply quote shows the placeholder. The sender may update only content and deleted_at; edited_at is set by the server (D-76). Edit, delete and react are disabled offline. Reactions are keyed (message_id, user_id, emoji) and removed by setting removed_at, never by DELETE. The palette is 8 emoji (thumbs up, heart, laughing, surprised, crying, folded hands, fire, party popper) and a user has at most 3 active reactions per message. | Closes OD-02 (default A with additions); a row DELETE reaches every subscriber unfiltered (Section 6.4). Settles PF-8 and part of PF-14. |
| D-23 | Read state is chat_members.last_read_seq, which never decreases; updates made offline apply on reconnection. Live display, the seen rule and the unread count: D-92. The unread count is the number of messages from other senders with seq above last_read_seq. | Closes OD-03 (default A, with a sequence number instead of a timestamp to match D-40). |
| D-24 | Archive and mute are columns on chat_members (archived, muted_until); folders use the folders and folder_chats tables. Folder names are at most 32 characters and a user has at most 10 folders. Changes need a connection. List, archive and folder rules: D-94. | Closes OD-04 (default A). Settles PF-9 and part of PF-14. |
| D-25 | Push is V2: a database webhook calls an Edge Function that calls the Expo Push API; tokens live in a push_tokens table; the payload carries no message text. Recipients, title and webhook authorization: D-96. | Closes OD-05 (default A, V2). Payload details: D-49. |
| D-26 | Users find each other by exact username. The creator of a group owns it and adds and removes members. Roles are owner, admin and member. Other users read username, display_name and avatar_url only; column privileges hide premium_flags (amended by D-87: premium_flags is readable). Lookup and profile visibility are tightened by D-42. Membership rules: D-70. | Closes OD-06 (default A). The exact-match rule was a client behavior (PF-20) until D-42 moved it to the server. |
| D-27 | Failed items offer manual Retry and Discard. The worker timer is 15 s (wake-up and attempt counting: D-77). Backoff has base 2 s, factor 2, cap 60 s and jitter ±20%. An item becomes failed after 8 attempts. The queue holds at most 200 items; a new item beyond that is refused with a visible message. Items of one chat are delivered strictly in order (row order versus upload order, queue count, Retry and Discard: D-93). The worker runs only in the foreground (D-109, D-117). | Closes OD-07 (default A with values). Settles PF-4. |
| D-28 | The automatic trigger is cellular 2G or 3G only. The large-media threshold is 5 MB. Photos are compressed to a 1280 px long edge at quality 0.7. Output format, no enlarging and evaluation timing: D-117. | Closes OD-08 (default A with values). Settles PF-5. |
| D-29 | Size caps: photos and voice notes 10 MB, videos and files 50 MB, with 1 MB = 1,048,576 bytes (D-116). Allowed types are listed in Section 5.5. Each cap is enforced by its bucket (D-75). Signed URLs last 1 hour. Uploads above 6 MB are resumable (TUS). The send limit is a token bucket with a burst of 200 and 60 messages per minute. Backup is a weekly manual database export. The Supabase region is the one closest to the owner and contacts (fixed as Tokyo by D-48). Amended by D-113 (signed URLs last 10 minutes), D-116 (units) and D-122 (bucket refill rates). | Closes OD-10 (default backup A, with values). Settles PF-3; the region is D-48 (PF-1). |
| D-30 | Message text supports CommonMark basics (bold, italic, code, links). The maximum length is 4096 characters. Empty and whitespace-only text is blocked unless the message has an attachment. Settles PF-6 and PF-13. |
| D-31 | A forwarded message shows no original sender; no schema change. Attachment forwarding moves to V2 (D-50). | Closes OD-12 (default A). |

| D-33 | Secret Chats are device-bound and mobile-only, represented as chats.type = secret (added in V2). Features that need server-readable content, such as search and reactions, are unavailable in them. The key exchange design is D-49. | Closes OD-14 (default A). |

| D-35 | Platform accessibility defaults plus WCAG 2.2 AA on web. The theme follows the system, with a manual override. | D-36 | Public sign-up is disabled; the owner creates accounts and the client signs in with shouldCreateUser set to false. | D-37 | Flow-level states and acceptance tests for V2 and V3 features are specified when each phase starts (M5-01, M6-01). Superseded by D-67: both are specified in v8, and M5-01 and M6-01 review them. | Closes OD-18 (default A). |
| D-38 | Members insert and remove only their own reactions and insert media rows only for messages they sent. Any signed-in user may insert a chat with created_by set to themselves. The chat creator manages chat_members (owner role and leaving: D-70). A member may update only last_read_seq, archived and muted_until on their own chat_members row. The chat_members insert branches are D-124. | Closes OD-19 (default A). Settles PF-12. |
| D-39 | media_id is generated on the client; storage_path is {chat_id}/{media_id}; an "already exists" upload result counts as success. | Closes OD-20 (default A). |
| D-40 | seq and sync_seq are assigned by the server from the per-chat counter chats.next_seq. seq is set once, at insert, and orders the chat; sync_seq takes a new value on every insert or update of a message, message_media row or reaction. Clients resync with sync_seq greater than their per-chat cursor. The counter row stays locked until the writing transaction commits, so sequence numbers become visible in commit order and no row falls behind a cursor. Supersedes D-18. | Closes OD-21 with option B (a server sequence) instead of the default A, because timestamps can commit out of order. Settles PF-15. |
| D-41 | Scale: typical use is 2 to 10 people, and the design runs unchanged up to the Supabase Free plan limits: 50,000 monthly active users, 200 concurrent Realtime connections, 100 Realtime messages per second, 100 channels per connection, 2 million Realtime messages per month, 500 MB database, 1 GB storage and 5 GB egress. Beyond them the answer is the Pro plan, not a redesign. | D-42 | Profiles are readable by their owner and by users who share a chat. | D-43 | Each client opens one Realtime connection and joins the typing channel only for the open chat. | D-44 | Policies call (select auth.uid()); chat_members(user_id, chat_id) and (chat_id, sync_seq) on the three synced tables are indexed; the chat list, message history and first sync are paged; unread counts come from one query. | Supabase RLS performance guidance (Assumption: recalled; checked at M1-04, D-85 item 9; fallback: the indexes and policy form follow the page as re-read). |

| D-46 | One token-bucket table keyed by user and action serves message sends (D-29), user lookup (30 per minute) and chat creation (20 per day). Capacities and refill rates: D-122. | Follows D-41; keeps a spammer from filling the database or listing accounts. |
| D-47 | Sign-in is email and password. | D-67 | V2 and V3 are specified in this version instead of when each phase starts. The V2 tables and columns are the V2 rows of Section 6.2 and the V2 policies of Section 10.5; the acceptance tests are T-23 to T-35 in Section 7.4. Messages gain kind (text, sticker, location or poll) and a payload checked per kind by a trigger: a sticker carries sticker_id from the bundled set, a location carries lat, lng and accuracy_m, and a poll message has a polls row with the same id. polls and poll_votes join the supabase_realtime publication; a vote is changed by UPDATE and withdrawn by removed_at, never deleted. push_tokens holds Expo push tokens with own-row access. Attachment forwarding (D-50) and profile editing (D-51) get their own tasks, M5-12 and M5-13. M5-01 and M6-01 become review tasks. Supersedes D-37. Server checks on kind and sticker ids: D-90, D-97; payload ranges, GIFs and send_poll: D-120; gates with a fallback: D-121. | Adopts option B of D-37 (specify now). v7 review: every V2 and V3 task pointed at a definition that did not exist yet, so nothing in M5 or M6 could be verified. |
| D-68 | Secret Chats are 1-on-1 between two Android users and text-only: no attachments, reactions, polls, stickers or forwarding. Each member creates an X25519 key pair for the chat on their device and inserts the public key into secret_keys once; the private key never leaves the device. content holds base64 of the nonce and the crypto_box ciphertext and may be up to 22,000 characters, because a 4096-character plaintext can be 16,384 bytes of UTF-8; the 4096-character rule of D-30 is applied to the plaintext on the client. Self-destruct timers (D-69) and typing indicators work as in other chats. A member who reinstalls the app or changes phone cannot read the chat and starts a new one. The web client does not list secret chats. Server checks and membership: D-90, D-91. | Closes the v7 gaps between ciphertext length and the D-30 length check, and on attachments in Secret Chats. Follows D-33 and D-49. |
| D-69 | A self-destructing message carries ttl_seconds (60, 3600, 86400 or 604800, D-59), chosen at send time; the server sets expires_at = created_at + ttl_seconds. Clients hide the message at expires_at. Its attachments stop being readable at expires_at, because the message_media and Storage read policies require a live parent message. A pg_cron job runs every minute and soft-deletes expired messages by UPDATE (content cleared, deleted_at set), so members receive the change through Postgres Changes and the stored text is gone within one minute of expiry. The monthly purge (D-75) deletes the files. Amended by D-111 (the cleanup runs as postgres and is the only update allowed after expiry) and D-113 (a signed URL issued before expiry lasts at most 10 minutes). | Closes the v7 gap that PF-18's monthly purge left self-destructed media readable for up to a month. D-14 forbids hard DELETE. |
| D-70 | Membership. A direct chat has exactly two members and Saved Messages one; their rows are fixed after creation, and a user hides an unwanted direct chat by archiving it. chats.direct_key holds the member ids in sorted order (the user id alone for Saved Messages), is unique and is set by a trigger, so a second direct chat with the same person fails and the client opens the existing one. In a group the owner adds members (owner and admins from V2, D-71), and a new member must already share a chat with the member who adds them, so nobody is put into a group by a stranger. Any member may leave a group by deleting their own chat_members row; when the owner leaves, the longest-standing remaining member becomes owner (an admin first, from V2; tie-break and last member: D-91). Policies check ownership by role owner, not by chats.created_by. Insert branches and the direct-chat exception: D-124. | Closes the v7 gaps: no way to leave a group, members added with no prior contact, and a creator able to remove the other side of a direct chat. Extends D-26 and D-38. |
| D-71 | V2 roles. The owner promotes members to admin and demotes admins. The owner and admins add members (rule of D-70) and remove members; an admin cannot remove or restrict the owner or another admin. Restrict sets chat_members.restricted_until to a time, or to infinity until lifted; a restricted member can read but cannot send messages, react or vote until then. Deleting other people's messages stays out of scope (D-22 keeps delete sender-only). Roles apply to groups only. Amended by D-112: restriction also blocks edits, re-added reactions and changed votes. | Gives "restrict" (D-09) a meaning and fixes the admin scope that M5-06 left to M5-01. |
| D-72 | Premium and profiles (V2). premium_flags is a text array holding any of badge, extra_reactions and premium_stickers, set only by the owner. badge shows a mark next to the display name; extra_reactions adds four emoji (star, hundred points, clapping hands, eyes) to that user's palette, checked by the reactions trigger; premium_stickers unlocks a marked subset of the bundled stickers for sending. Animated profile pictures are dropped (storage and egress), "no ads" does not apply, and the app icon is a build-time choice (D-32). Profile editing (D-51) comes with avatars: a private avatars bucket, path {user_id}/{uuid}, at most 512 KB of JPEG, PNG or WebP, written by its owner and readable by anyone allowed to read the profile. find_user does not return avatar_url (D-88). In the MVP avatar_url stays empty and clients show initials. Enforcement of the extra emoji: D-119. | Closes the v7 gaps that avatar_url had no source and that M5-08's retained items were unnamed. |
| D-73 | V3 calls are voice and video in direct chats only, on mobile and web. WebRTC uses Google's public STUN server and no TURN relay, because the free terms of TURN services are unverified and a relay would carry all call media. A call that cannot connect directly within 20 seconds ends with the message "Call could not connect on this network". Signalling (offer, answer, ICE candidates, hang-up) uses the private Realtime Broadcast channel call:<chat_id>, authorized like typing channels. Mobile uses react-native-webrtc with its Expo config plugin. Assumption: the plugin supports the Expo SDK in use; checked at M6-01, and if it does not, calls are web-only. Invitations and the browser-only test fallback: D-98. Calls are not recorded and there is no call history. | Closes the TURN question that D-49 left conditional, keeps Goal 1.1 (free to operate) and removes the signalling Assumption of M6-02. |
| D-74 | The monthly media copy uses a supabase-js script that lists both media buckets through the Storage API and downloads every object not yet in the local copy, run from the owner's computer with the secret key. The S3-compatible endpoint is not needed. Amends D-52 and the S3 item of D-65. | Removes the unverified Free plan S3 Assumption from the backup path; the Storage API is available on every plan. |
| D-75 | Chat media lives in two private buckets by size class: chat-media-small (photos and voice notes; 10 MB; the image and audio types of 5.5) and chat-media-large (videos and files; 50 MB; every type of 5.5). The bucket follows message_media.media_type and the path stays {chat_id}/{media_id} (D-39), so every cap and type rule of D-29 is enforced by Storage as well as by the client. Upload requires chat membership. Download and signed URLs also require a message_media row for the object whose message is neither deleted nor expired, so a deleted message's files stop being readable at once (amended by D-113: new downloads and signed URLs stop at once; a URL issued earlier lasts at most 10 minutes). The monthly purge deletes, through the Storage API, the files of deleted or expired messages and objects with no message_media row that are older than 24 hours. Amends D-29, D-39 and D-55 (PF-18). | Closes two v7 conflicts: 5.5 claimed per-type caps on the server that a single 50 MB bucket cannot enforce, and the message_media read rule hid deleted rows while their files stayed downloadable. |
| D-76 | Message and reaction updates. On its own message a client may write only content (edit) and deleted_at (delete, any non-null value); edited_at is not writable. The server sets edited_at and deleted_at to now(), clears content on delete, rejects any update of a deleted message and rejects an update that changes neither column. A reaction is re-added by clearing removed_at, and that update is subject to the same limit of 3 active reactions per user per message as an insert. reactions.emoji has a CHECK constraint for the D-22 palette, extended in V2 by D-72. Amends D-22. The CHECK and trigger split for the extra emoji: D-119. | Closes the v7 ambiguities on client-written timestamps and on re-added reactions bypassing the limit. |
| D-77 | Queue retries. Each queue item stores next_attempt_at. Connectivity events and the 15-second timer only wake the worker; an item is attempted when its next_attempt_at has passed. retry_count increases only for an attempt made while the device reported a connection that failed with a network or server error. When a connection returns, every queued item becomes due at once without resetting retry_count. A rate-limit rejection (SQLSTATE DG429, with the retry time in seconds in the error detail) is not an attempt: the item keeps its place and waits until that time, so a full queue drains however long the refill takes. Amends D-27 and the rejection rule of M3-08. | Closes the v7 ambiguities on how the LS-3 timer and LS-7 backoff interact, and on rate-limit rejections exhausting the 8 attempts of a strictly ordered queue. |
| D-78 | The forced password change is enforced by the server. New accounts and owner resets set app_metadata.must_change_password = true, which users cannot edit. Every table policy, the Storage policies and the Realtime authorization call the helper is_active_user(), which is false while auth.users carries the flag (D-86), so the account can sign in and change its password but read or write nothing else. A BEFORE UPDATE trigger on auth.users removes the flag when encrypted_password changes, and the client refreshes its session after updateUser so the new JWT lacks it. The reset script sets the new password first and the flag second. Assumption: Supabase permits this trigger on auth.users as it does the insert trigger of M1-03; checked in M1-03; the fallback is the server-side check of D-86, not the client screen of D-60. is_active_user() reads auth.users, not the session token (D-86). Amends D-60. | Closes the v7 gap that users can edit their own user_metadata, so the D-60 flag enforced nothing. |
| D-79 | A banned account loses access at once: is_active_user() (D-78) is also false while auth.users.banned_until is in the future, and the membership helpers use it, so database reads, Storage and Realtime authorization stop before the account's access token expires. Amended by D-114: a channel joined before the ban re-checks only on a new JWT, so it stops within the 15-minute JWT lifetime. The helper reads auth.users directly (D-86). Its messages stay (D-61). Amends D-61. | Closes the v7 gap that a banned user's access token stayed valid until it expired. |
| D-80 | Catalog features out of scope, with D-15 and Section 3.3, listed in full: channels (channel is not a stored value, D-91), message search, pinned messages, drafts, scheduled messages, send silently, round video messages, custom sticker packs made by users, GIF search, quiz-mode polls, live location, contact sharing, media auto-download settings, group voice chats and group video calls, two-factor authentication, screenshot detection, privacy controls for phone number, last seen and profile photo, blocking users (the owner bans abusive accounts, D-61), active sessions management, member permissions beyond restrict (D-71), slow mode, invite links, public usernames for groups, channel statistics, discussion groups, topics, join requests, chat themes and wallpapers, multiple accounts in one app, QR codes and nearby users. | D-86 | is_active_user() is a SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE helper in the private schema that reads auth.users, not the session token. It returns false while raw_app_meta_data.must_change_password is true, while banned_until is in the future, or while the user has a row in private.disabled_users. Policies call it as (select private.is_active_user()), so it runs once per statement (D-44), and a password change or a ban takes effect on the next request without waiting for a token refresh. If Supabase refuses the BEFORE UPDATE trigger of D-78, create-user and the reset script copy auth.users.encrypted_password into private.initial_passwords after setting the password, and the helper is false while the two are equal. If the Admin API ban does not set banned_until as documented, scripts/disable-user inserts the user into private.disabled_users. Amends D-61, D-78 and D-79. | v8 made D-78 depend on token claims that change only on refresh, and fell back to a client-side screen, which Section 5 does not accept as a control. |
| D-87 | premium_flags is readable by everyone who may read the profile, so a badge can be shown to other members. It stays writable only by the owner through the secret key, because no client may update it. Column privileges no longer hide it. | D-88 | find_user returns user_id and display_name only. avatar_url is left out because the avatars bucket is readable only by users who may read the profile (D-72), so a stranger could not load it. Amends D-42. | v8 had find_user return a path its caller was not allowed to read. |
| D-89 | Profiles. username matches ^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{4,31}$ (5 to 32 characters, lowercase) and is unique; display_name is 1 to 64 characters after trimming. Both are CHECK constraints, and create-user validates them first. Lengths count Unicode code points (D-116). In the MVP clients have no UPDATE privilege on profiles (D-51); M5-13 grants UPDATE of username, display_name and avatar_url on the caller's own row. A message whose sender's profile the caller can no longer read, because the sender left the chat, shows the sender as "Former member". Amends D-38 and D-51. | v8 granted a profile update in the MVP that D-51 defers, and gave usernames no format. |
| D-90 | Message integrity is enforced by triggers. messages.attachment_count (0 to 10) is written at insert and cannot change. A message whose kind is text and whose attachment_count is 0 must not have blank content. A message_media insert is rejected once the message has attachment_count rows. reply_to must name a message in the same chat. content is at most 4096 characters, or in a secret chat at most 22,000 characters of base64 (^[A-Za-z0-9+/=]*$), so plaintext cannot be stored there by mistake. An update is rejected once expires_at has passed. On message_media the trigger sets storage_path to {chat_id}/{media_id} and requires an object at that path in the bucket of the media_type, whose owner_id is the caller and whose declared type matches the media_type (photo image/*, voice audio/*, video video/*, file any type of 5.5). Clients show one placeholder for each attachment that has no row yet. Assumption: Storage compares allowed_mime_types with the declared Content-Type, not the file bytes; accepted, because every reader is a chat member and the apps never execute a file. Amends D-30, D-39, D-68 and D-75. Amended by D-111 (the expiry cleanup), D-116 (counting) and D-120 (payload ranges and poll rows). | Closes the v8 gaps that the empty-text rule had no server check, that the 4096-character check contradicted the 22,000-character secret-chat limit, that sticker, location and poll messages have no text, and that a media row could point at any object. |
| D-91 | Chat types and membership details. chats.type has a CHECK for direct and group, and V2 adds secret; channel is out of scope (D-80) and is not a stored value. A secret chat has exactly two members, the second of whom must already share a direct chat with the first; its direct_key is null, so a new secret chat can replace a lost one. When a group owner leaves, ownership passes to the member with the earliest joined_at, ties broken by the lowest user_id (an admin first, from V2). When the last member leaves, the chat row and its messages stay, unreachable (D-63). A client that no longer receives a chat in its chat list fetch deletes that chat's local cache and sync state. Amends D-21, D-68 and D-70. | v8 let a client insert the reserved channel type, did not say who may join a secret chat, and left the ownership tie and removed members' caches open. |
| D-92 | Read receipts and typing. After writing last_read_seq the client sends a Broadcast event read on chat:<chat_id>; members with that chat open re-read the chat's chat_members rows through RLS, so the state shown always comes from the database and a forged event changes nothing. Other clients refresh on open and foreground (M2-05). A message shows as seen in a direct chat when the other member's last_read_seq is at least its seq, and in a group when at least one other member's is. The unread count excludes deleted and expired messages. Typing events are advisory: the payload names the typist and is not verified, so a member could fake another member's indicator; accepted, because nothing is stored. Amends D-23, D-43 and D-57. | Closes the v8 gaps that read status changed only on open or foreground and that "seen" in groups was an Assumption. |
| D-93 | Queue order and actions. Strict order applies to inserting message rows: the rows of one chat are inserted in enqueue order. Attachments upload after their row is stored and may finish in any order, and a deferred or retrying upload never blocks later items of the chat. Every item not yet sent (queued, sending or failed) counts toward the 200-item limit. Retry sets retry_count to 0 and makes the item due at once. Discard removes an item whose row the server never stored; when the row exists, Discard soft-deletes it first, so it needs a connection and is disabled offline (D-22). An interrupted upload restarts from the beginning. A connection that is neither Wi-Fi nor cellular counts as Normal in LS-5. Amends D-27 and D-77. | Closes the v8 conflict between strict per-chat order and Wi-Fi deferral, and the open meaning of Retry, Discard and the queue count. |
| D-94 | Chat list and organization. The main list shows chats that are not archived, ordered by the created_at of their newest message, else by chats.created_at, with ties by chat_id. The Archive view lists archived chats in the same order, and a new message does not unarchive a chat. A folder lists its chats that are not archived. A muted chat stays in place with an indicator. Folder names are 1 to 32 characters (CHECK) and a trigger refuses an eleventh folder. Deleting a folder deletes its folder_chats rows by ON DELETE CASCADE, never the chats. The archive, mute and folder controls are disabled offline (D-24). Amends D-24. | Replaces the "Telegram-like rules" Assumption of M4-01 and the ordering Assumption of F2. |
| D-95 | Checkable cross-cutting states. A wait over 300 ms shows a loading indicator. Every error other than a send failure shows its text from the strings module, with Retry where the action can be repeated. Every interactive element has an accessibility label, checked by eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y on web and by a test that renders each mobile screen and lists pressables without accessibilityLabel. The theme tokens give text a contrast of at least 4.5:1 in both themes, checked by a script. On web the Tab order follows the visual order. UI text is English. Amends D-35, D-54 and D-83. | Turns the derived checks of M4-02 and M4-03 into pass criteria (T-40). |
| D-96 | Push details (V2). A database webhook on messages INSERT only (edits and deletes send nothing) calls the Edge Function with the header Authorization: Bearer <PUSH_WEBHOOK_SECRET>, an Edge Function secret; any other call gets 401. Recipients are the chat's members other than the sender who pass is_active_user(), whose muted_until is null or past, and who have a push_tokens row. The title is the group title, or the sender's display_name in a direct or secret chat; the body is "New message" and the data carries only chat_id (D-49). The app registers a token only after the OS notification permission is granted (POST_NOTIFICATIONS on Android 13 and later). Amends D-25, D-49 and D-67. | Closes the v8 gaps on mute (an Assumption in M5-10), on how the Edge Function knows a call is genuine, and on the title of a direct chat, which has no title. |

| D-98 | Call invitations (V3). While in the foreground, each signed-in client joins the private Broadcast channel user:<own user_id>. A caller sends the invitation there, and both sides then use call:<chat_id> (D-73). Realtime authorization lets only that user read user:<user_id>, and lets only an active member of a direct chat shared with that user send to it. A call rings only while the callee's app is open in the foreground; no push is sent for calls. An unanswered call ends after 30 seconds with "No answer". If react-native-webrtc fails at M6-01, calls are web-only and T-34 and T-35 run between two browsers. | D-109 | Section 11 is the screen inventory of the user Android app (AND-01 to AND-24), the user web app (WEB-01 to WEB-20) and the admin web (ADM-01 to ADM-08). It fixes these UI rules, which no earlier section set. (1) Mute offers 1 hour, 8 hours, 1 week and Until I unmute (muted_until = infinity). (2) Restrict offers 1 hour, 1 day, 1 week and Until lifted (V2, D-71). (3) A group title is 1 to 64 characters after trimming, and other chat types have no title; a CHECK on chats.title enforces it. (4) In the MVP, Forward is hidden on a message with no text, because only text is forwarded (D-50) and a blank forward would be rejected (D-90). | D-110 | Account deletion (closes the conditional of D-108). Deleting an account, only on request (D-61), erases the person's data with no hard DELETE on a published table. packages/admin-core, with the secret key: (1) soft-deletes by UPDATE every message the user sent that is neither deleted nor expired (content cleared, deleted_at set); (2) sets removed_at on the user's active reactions and poll votes; (3) deletes the user's chat_members rows in groups, with ownership passing per D-91, and the user's push_tokens rows and avatar object; (4) sets username to deleted_ plus the first 8 hex digits of the user id, display_name to "Deleted account", avatar_url to null and premium_flags to an empty array; (5) bans the account permanently (banned_until = infinity and a private.disabled_users row), sets its email to <user_id>@deleted.invalid and its password to a random value that is never shown. The auth.users row and the rows of direct chats and Saved Messages stay, so no foreign key cascades and every change reaches other members through sync_seq (D-40). The audit row records the counts. Enforced by admin-core; tested by T-53. Amends D-61 and D-108. | v11 review: a cascading delete would hard-delete messages, which D-14 forbids and which resync by sync_seq never reports to other members' caches. |
| D-111 | Expiry cleanup. The D-90 rule that rejects an update after expires_at applies to client requests (database roles authenticated and anon). private.expire_messages(), run every minute by pg_cron as postgres, sets content = '' and deleted_at = now() on messages whose expires_at has passed and deleted_at is null, and takes a new sync_seq like any update. The messages UPDATE trigger allows exactly that change from a non-client role; any other change after expiry is rejected for every role. Enforced by the trigger; tested by T-29. Amends D-69 and D-90. | v11 conflict: D-69 cleans up by UPDATE while D-90 rejected every update after expiry, so T-29 could not pass. |
| D-112 | Restriction scope (V2). Until restricted_until, a restricted member cannot send, edit, react, re-add a reaction, vote or change a vote. They can still delete their own messages, remove their own reactions and withdraw their own vote. Enforced by RLS: the INSERT policies of messages, reactions and poll_votes, and the WITH CHECK of their UPDATE policies, require that the caller is not restricted, except an update that only sets deleted_at (messages) or removed_at (reactions, poll_votes). Tested by T-30. Amends D-71. | v11 gap: 10.5 restricted only INSERT, while a reaction is re-added and a vote is changed by UPDATE. |
| D-113 | Signed URL lifetime. Signed URLs last 10 minutes instead of 1 hour. A signed URL stays valid until its expiry whatever the policies say (Supabase Storage serving documentation, checked 31 October 2026), so the Storage SELECT policy of D-75 stops new downloads and new signed URLs at once, and a URL issued before a delete or an expiry keeps working for at most 10 minutes. Uploads set cacheControl to 600 seconds so no cached response outlives the URL. Clients request a new URL when one expires (AND-09, WEB-10). Enforced by the Storage policy and the lifetime the client requests from the shared config module; tested by T-11 and T-29. Amends D-29, D-69 and D-75. | v11 conflict: D-75 promised that a deleted message's files stop being readable at once, but an issued signed URL is not re-checked. |
| D-114 | Realtime revocation window. Realtime caches a client's channel authorization per connection and re-checks it only on a channel join or a new access token; a client that sends no new token is disconnected when its JWT expires (Supabase Realtime Authorization documentation, checked 31 October 2026). A ban or a forced password change therefore stops database reads, Storage and new channel joins at once (D-79, D-86) and stops an already-joined channel within the JWT lifetime. The Auth JWT expiry is 900 seconds on the hosted project and in config.toml, and ADM-08 checks it. Accepted, because Broadcast carries no message content. Tested by T-47. Amends D-79. | v11 gap: D-79 claimed Realtime authorization stops at once, which the caching rule contradicts. |
| D-115 | Web deployment. Contacts use the web app at its Vercel Hobby production address (*.vercel.app), deployed from the main branch of the private repository (D-62). next dev on the owner's computer is for development only. Vercel Hobby is free for personal, non-commercial use (Vercel fair use guidelines), which fits 1.2. Closes the "or run locally" choice of 6.5, 10.2, M0-09 and R-5. Amends D-53. | v11 left the web host as a choice; contacts cannot reach a local server. |
| D-116 | Units and text measurement, for every limit. (1) A character is a Unicode code point, as Postgres char_length counts it; clients count with Array.from(text).length, never String.length. (2) Trimming removes leading and trailing space, tab, line feed, carriage return, form feed and vertical tab, with the same character class in Postgres and JavaScript; blank means empty after trimming. (3) 1 KB = 1,024 bytes and 1 MB = 1,048,576 bytes; bucket limits are set in bytes (10,485,760; 52,428,800; 524,288), as are the 5 MB threshold (5,242,880) and the 6 MB TUS threshold (6,291,456). (4) The client accepts an email that matches ^[^\s@]+@[^\s@]+\.[^\s@]+$; Supabase Auth's own validation is the server rule. Enforced by the CHECK constraints and triggers of D-89, D-90, D-94 and D-109, by the bucket limits and by the shared validation module (M2-01); tested by T-39 and T-52. Amends D-28, D-29, D-30, D-89, D-94 and D-109. | v11 ambiguity: Postgres and JavaScript count and trim text differently, and "MB" had two readings. |
| D-117 | Low Signal evaluation and compression output. (1) The Force toggle is recorded on each item at enqueue (LS-6); the LS-5 row is evaluated at each delivery attempt from the network state at that moment. (2) A photo is compressed at its first attempt in the Automatic or Forced row; the compressed file replaces the original in the item and is reused on every later attempt, and message_media.compressed records it. (3) Compression outputs JPEG at quality 0.7 with the long edge scaled down to at most 1280 px; a smaller image is re-encoded without resizing and never enlarged. (4) The worker runs only in the foreground (D-109), so "sent immediately", "as soon as the device reports a Wi-Fi connection" and the LS-3 timer apply while the app or tab is in the foreground. Enforced in the client queue (packages/shared); tested by T-03, T-08 and T-49. Amends D-27, D-28 and D-109 and the text of LS-3, LS-5 and LS-6. | v11 ambiguity: LS-6 snapshotted the toggle but not the network row, T-08's result depended on the cellular generation, and LS-3 and LS-5 did not state the foreground rule of D-109. |
| D-118 | Typing over Broadcast only. Typing and read hints use Broadcast; Presence is not used (online status is not built, D-55), so the Realtime authorization policies allow the broadcast extension only and refuse a Presence join. The indicator shows "<name> is typing..." for one typist and "<n> people are typing..." for more, and hides 5 seconds after a typist's last event or when that typist's message arrives (D-57). Enforced by Realtime authorization (Presence) and the client (display); tested by T-14 and T-51. Amends D-14, D-43, D-57 and the PF-16 note. | v11 ambiguity: Sections 3.2, 6.1 and 10.5 said Broadcast/Presence although nothing uses Presence, and the display of several typists was undefined. |
| D-119 | Extra reactions (V2). From M5-08 the reactions.emoji CHECK lists the 12 emoji of D-22 and D-72. The reactions trigger rejects one of the 4 extra emoji on an insert, or on an update that clears removed_at, when the user's premium_flags lacks extra_reactions at that moment. Removing the flag leaves active extra reactions in place; they count toward the limit of 3 and cannot be re-added once removed. Enforced by the CHECK and the trigger; tested by T-31. Amends D-72 and D-76. | v11 ambiguity: a CHECK cannot depend on a per-user flag, and revoking the flag had no rule. |
| D-120 | Message kinds (V2). (1) GIFs are stickers: a bundled GIF is sent as kind sticker with a sticker_id of D-97, and the picker shows GIFs in their own tab by file type. (2) The payload trigger checks each kind: text has an empty payload; sticker has only sticker_id; location has lat from -90 to 90, lng from -180 to 180 and accuracy_m from 0 to 100,000, all numbers; poll has an empty payload. (3) A poll is sent through send_poll(message_id, chat_id, question, options), SECURITY INVOKER so RLS applies, which inserts the message and its polls row in one transaction; a constraint trigger, DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED, rejects at commit a poll message without its polls row. The queue sends a poll item through send_poll, and a repeat with the same message_id counts as delivered (LS-8). (4) poll_votes.option_index is from 0 to the number of options minus 1 (trigger). Tested by T-25, T-26 and T-27. Amends D-67 and D-97. | v11 gaps: GIFs had no kind, the payload check named no ranges, and nothing guaranteed that a poll message has its polls row. |
| D-121 | Gates with a fallback. A test that a D-85 fallback makes not applicable counts as passed for its gate when the test log records the fallback that applied and the check result that triggered it: T-23 when Firebase demands billing (D-85 item 4), and T-34 and T-35 run between two browsers when react-native-webrtc fails (D-85 item 6). Amends 3.1, D-67 and D-85, and the exit conditions of M5 and M6. | v11 ambiguity: the gates required every test to pass while D-85 allowed tests to be recorded as not applicable. |
| D-122 | Token buckets. Each bucket has a capacity and a continuous refill: message sends 200 tokens, 1 per second; user lookup 30 tokens, 1 per 2 seconds; chat creation 20 tokens, 1 per 72 minutes (20 per 24 hours). A request takes one token; with none left it raises DG429 with the seconds until the next token (D-77). "Per minute" and "per day" in 5.5 and Section 8 mean these refill rates, not calendar windows. Enforced by the triggers on messages and chats and inside find_user (M3-08); tested by T-21. Amends D-29 and D-46. | v11 ambiguity: "20 per day" could be a calendar day in any time zone or a rolling window. |
| D-123 | Capacity actions. The Overview bar of a limit turns amber at 80% (D-45), and the owner acts the same day: database or file storage, run the purge (ADM-06) and record the sizes before and after in the audit log; Realtime peak connections, switch message delivery from Postgres Changes to database Broadcast behind the packages/shared interface (D-43); Realtime messages or egress, no change before the monthly reset, and the owner tells the contacts. The Pro plan stays the only escape hatch beyond the Free plan (D-41). Enforced by the admin web Overview and the runbook (M4-05); tested by T-48. Amends D-43 and D-45. | v11 gap: D-45 said "act at 80%" with no action, and 6.4 left the switch to Broadcast at "if the owner outgrows it". |
| D-124 | Member row inserts. The chat_members INSERT policy has two branches. (1) The creator's own first row: user_id = the caller, role owner, chats.created_by = the caller and the chat has no members yet. (2) Any other row: inserted by the chat's owner (or a group admin from V2, D-71) with role member. The shared-chat rule of D-70 applies to groups only; a direct chat's second row needs only the two-member limit, because find_user is how a person is reached. Enforced by RLS and the chat_members trigger; tested by T-38. Amends D-38 and D-70. | v11 ambiguity: the policy said "the chat owner adds members" without saying how the first owner row is allowed. |
| D-125 | Verification on 31 October 2026 against the Supabase documentation. The Free plan includes 2 million Realtime messages and 200 Realtime peak connections per month, 50,000 monthly active users, a 500 MB database, 1 GB of storage and 5 GB of egress (Supabase billing documentation). Free projects with low database activity over 7 days are paused after a warning email a week before and can be restored for 90 days (Supabase project pausing documentation). Signed URLs stay valid until expiry (D-113). Realtime authorization is cached per connection (D-114). The 7.1 Assumptions on the monthly Realtime figure and the pause rule are removed. D-85 gains items 14 to 17 (netinfo version, Broadcast at scale, browser parity, sign-in rate limit). Amends D-41 and D-85. | Supabase documentation search through the Supabase MCP server, 31 October 2026. |
| D-126 | Corrections in v12. D-78 and M1-02 no longer say that is_active_user() reads the JWT (D-86). D-80 no longer calls channel a reserved stored value (D-91). LS-3, LS-5 and LS-6 state the foreground worker and the evaluation timing (D-109, D-117). Test account D is renamed G and also joins the fixture group A+B+G, so D names only the device. 10.6 builds the admin web before the V2 steps, and the 10.9 critical path includes M4-08, which the MVP gate needs. 3.2, 6.1, 10.5 and 10.10 say Broadcast instead of Broadcast/Presence (D-118). 5.1 and 5.3 state the Realtime and signed-URL windows (D-113, D-114). 7.1 cites the Supabase billing and pausing pages instead of Assumptions (D-125). D-47, 7.1, PR-2 and PR-14 cite their sources. The PF-16 and PF-21 notes and PR-20 match D-110 and D-118. T-17 and T-48 replace the derived checks for MVP attachment forwarding and backups. T-05 also checks chats and chat_members rows. The Assumptions of 3.2, D-04, D-43, D-44 and M5-10 name their check point and fallback. M1-09, 10.5, 10.7, PF-18 and the 10.1 rows of D-29, D-53 and D-71 match D-112, D-113 and D-115, and R-7 deletes an account through the admin web (D-110). The cover, footer, D-20 and PF-24 name v12; the MVP gate runs T-01 to T-22 and T-36 to T-53. Amends D-78, D-80 and D-101. | v12 review of the cross-references in v11. |
| D-127 | Design system. Section 12 is the single visual and interaction specification of the user Android app, the user web app and the admin web: tokens, icons, components CMP-01 to CMP-31, screen templates, navigation, responsive and accessibility rules, and the implementation mapping. It adds no feature and no backend behavior. Instagram is a pattern reference only; no Instagram logo, wordmark, icon, font, screenshot or exact color is used, and every description of Instagram in 12.1 is marked Assumption. Every quoted UI string in Section 12 that no earlier section fixes is the initial value of its key in the strings module (D-35, D-95); strings fixed earlier are quoted unchanged. Free and open-licensed assets only: Inter and JetBrains Mono (SIL Open Font License 1.1), Lucide (ISC), the platform emoji fonts. Amends D-35. | Product owner request for a design system (v13) with Instagram as the pattern reference and free, open-licensed fonts and icons; Goal 1.1. |
| D-128 | Navigation. The user apps keep the navigation of 11.2 and 11.3: the chat list is home and its header holds New chat, New group and Settings; there is no bottom tab bar on Android and no left navigation sidebar in the user web app. The admin web keeps the left sidebar of 11.4. Android navigation uses an expo-router stack with the route files of 12.6. The user web and admin web use the Next.js App Router routes of 12.6 (D-100); dialogs and overlays are state, not routes. | Conflict between the v13 request (bottom tab bar on mobile, left sidebar on web, after Instagram) and AND-03 and WEB-03, which are binding; resolved in favor of Section 11. |
| D-129 | Identity and color. The product name is written DoloGram and set as a wordmark in Inter Bold. Every text pair reaches at least 4.5:1 and every non-text pair at least 3:1 in both themes. | D-131 | Icons and emoji. | D-133 | Forwarded messages render as ordinary bubbles with no "Forwarded" label and no original sender, because a forward is a new message with copied text and no stored attribution (D-31). Toasts last 4000 ms, or 6000 ms with an action. Loading indicators appear only after 300 ms (D-95). | Platform accessibility defaults (D-54); D-95 loading threshold. |

| D-136 | Accessibility values. Touch targets are at least 48 by 48 dp on Android, 44 by 44 px on the user web and 32 by 32 px on the admin web. The keyboard focus ring is 2 px color.focus.ring with a 2 px offset. Labels, roles, focus order and announcements follow 12.8. | D-137 | Admin web components. The admin web uses the shared tokens and components at compact sizes and adds CMP-24 to CMP-31: data table (50 rows per page, fixed order), filter bar, form with the one-time password panel, confirmation dialog with a typed confirmation for Delete account, audit view, usage bar and job panel, the "unavailable" panel and status pills. Usage bars state their percentage in text and add an icon at 80% and 100%, so color is never the only signal. | Sections 6.6 and 11.4 define the admin screens and their rules but no visual components; D-45, D-104 to D-108 and D-123. |
| D-138 | Implementation mapping. Tokens live once in packages/shared/src/theme/tokens.ts and icons.ts; tokens.css for the web apps is generated from them and a test fails when it is out of date. Android reads tokens through ThemeProvider and useTheme(); web through --dg- CSS custom properties with data-theme on <html>; the admin web uses the system media query only (D-109). Components share PascalCase names and live in apps/mobile/src/components, apps/web/src/components and apps/admin/src/components; web styling uses CSS Modules. ESLint and Stylelint reject color literals outside the theme module, and a Vitest contrast test checks the pairs of 12.2. Amends D-100 (adds the theme module to packages/shared). | M4-02 requires one token set and a static check for color literals; 7.1 Client drift. |

Android: a bottom tab bar (CMP-32) on the root screens with Chats (AND-03), New (a sheet with New chat, AND-10, and New group, AND-11) and Profile (the user's avatar, opening Settings, AND-14), hidden on every other screen including the chat; Settings becomes a tab root with no back button, and the back button there returns to Chats. No time inside a bubble: centered time rows at the start of each day and after a gap of 60 minutes, and the time of one message on tap (Android, web below 768 px) or hover (web from 768 px). "edited" shows under the bubble. Status: queued, sending, "sending (low signal)", "waiting for Wi-Fi" and failed show under every affected bubble in words; "Sent" or "Seen" shows under the last own message only, so an own message with no caption is sent; chat rows keep the status icon; every bubble's accessibility label keeps its status. 10. Implementation Plan
This section turns Sections 1 to 9 and the screens of Section 11 into an ordered build plan. A developer MUST be able to build DoloGram from it without asking questions, and a tester MUST be able to verify each task from its acceptance criteria. It builds only what Sections 1 to 9 and 11 define and adds no feature. It contains no effort estimates; ordering and dependencies take their place.
10.1 Inputs, open decisions and flags
Inputs
Source of truth: Sections 1 to 9 and 11 of this document, plus the official documentation for Expo, Next.js and Supabase.
Decisions: OD-01 to OD-23 are closed in v4 and v5, v8 closes the deferred items of v7, v9 closes what v8 left conditional, v10 adds the admin web, v11 adds the screen breakdown, v12 closes the conditional item, gaps and ambiguities of v11, and the plan applies D-21 to D-126. Each step that depends on one names its D number. The table below says what the plan applies and what changes if the owner reverses it later.
Conventions: MUST, SHOULD and MAY have the meaning defined in RFC 2119, and the conventions on the cover page apply (OD-nn, D-nn, LS-n, Assumption:). Section 10 adds four identifiers: M0 to M6 (milestones), Mn-nn (tasks), PF-n (a gap or conflict in Sections 1 to 9 that blocks or constrains a task) and PR-n (a risk introduced by this plan).
Rules: a task MUST NOT be marked done until its acceptance criteria pass. A task blocked by a flag MUST be listed as blocked, not skipped. A value the owner supplies later MUST be recorded in Section 9 as a decision.
Decisions the plan applies (OD-09 was closed to D-19; OD-01 to OD-23 are closed in v4 and v5; D-56 to D-65 close the residual items of v5; D-67 to D-84 close the deferred items, gaps and ambiguities of v7; D-85 to D-101 close the conditional items, gaps and ambiguities of v8; D-102 to D-108 add the admin web; D-109 adds the screen rules of Section 11; D-110 to D-126 close the items of v11)
| Decision | Applied in this plan | Steps that depend on it | If the owner reverses it |
| D-21 (OD-01) | Other catalog features out of scope; app lock in V2; channels have no behavior. | M1-01, M4-06, M5-09 | B: each listed feature needs a phase, a Section 3.2 row and tasks; chats.type = channel needs defined behavior; if app lock moves to MVP, M5-09 moves into M4. |
| D-22 (OD-02) | Sender-only edit and delete, no time limit, delete for everyone, content cleared, placeholder shown; offline edit, delete and react disabled; reactions removed by UPDATE of removed_at; palette of 8, at most 3 per user per message. | M1-02, M1-03, M2-09, M2-10 | B: Telegram-style options add a time-limit check in the UPDATE policy, delete-for-self and admin-delete policies, and queue item types for offline edits and reactions. |
| D-23 (OD-03) | chat_members.last_read_seq; offline updates applied on reconnection. | M1-01, M2-11 | B: a per-message receipts table with policies, a publication entry and a UI count. C: no persisted read state; the read-status part of F6 and T-04 is dropped. |
| D-24 (OD-04) | archived and muted_until on chat_members plus folders and folder_chats; folder name at most 32 characters, at most 10 folders. | M1-01, M4-01 | B: device-local only; drop the columns and tables, store settings in the local store, and F9's cross-device sync no longer holds. |
| D-25 (OD-05) | V2; database webhook, Edge Function, Expo Push API; tokens in a new table; payload has no message text. | M5-10 | B: no push in v1; remove M5-10, the F11 flow and the FCM setup. |
| D-26 (OD-06) | Exact-username lookup; creator is owner and manages members; roles owner, admin, member. | M1-01, M1-02, M2-07 | B: lookup by email exposes email addresses through profiles. C: QR codes need a scanner and a code format. |
| D-27 (OD-07) | Manual retry and discard; timer 15 s; backoff base 2 s, factor 2, cap 60 s, jitter ±20%; 8 attempts; 200 items; strict order per chat. | M2-03, M3-08 | B: automatic retry only; failed items stay visible with no actions. |
| D-28 (OD-08) | Cellular-generation trigger only; threshold 5 MB; photos 1280 px long edge at quality 0.7. | M3-05, M3-07 | A latency-based trigger adds a latency probe, a threshold and a test case to M3-05 and M3-07. |
| D-29 (OD-10) | Caps 10 MB (photos, voice notes) and 50 MB (video, files); allowed types in 5.5; signed URL 10 minutes (D-113); TUS above 6 MB; rate limit burst 200 and 60 per minute; weekly manual backup; region Tokyo (ap-northeast-1) (D-48). | M0-03, M1-06, M3-01, M3-08, M4-05 | Backup B: accept the risk, skip the restore drill in M4-05. Choosing resumable (TUS) uploads changes the upload call in M3-01. |
| D-30 (OD-11) | CommonMark basics; maximum 4096 characters; empty or whitespace-only text blocked unless the message has an attachment. | M2-01, M2-06 | B: a Telegram-style formatting set is not standard Markdown; both renderers need custom syntax. |
| D-31 (OD-12) | No attribution, no schema change. | M2-09, M3-04 | B: add an attribution column, a policy check and a UI label. |
| D-32 (OD-13) | Drop profiles.status; premium flags set by the owner; vague items dropped; app icon chosen at build time. | M1-01, M5-08 | B: keep status and the items as written; their meaning must be defined before M5-08. |
| D-33 (OD-14) | Device-bound, mobile only; chats.type secret added in V2. | M5-07 | B: shared keys across devices need a key-sync design and make web Secret Chats possible. |
| D-34 (OD-15) | 1-on-1 calls only; Google STUN and no TURN (D-49, D-73). | M5-04, M6-01 | B: choose now; M5-04 and M6-01 are unblocked earlier. |
| D-35 (OD-16) | Platform defaults (the WCAG AA review is dropped, D-54); system theme with manual override; latency 2 s at the 95th percentile; web breakpoint 768 px. | M2-01, M2-08, M4-02, M4-03, M4-04 | B: project-specific standards replace the defaults in M4-02 and M4-03. |
| D-36 (OD-17) | Superseded by D-47: owner-created accounts, email and password sign-in, no outgoing email. | M0-05, M0-06, M1-08, M2-04 | A custom SMTP provider would be needed if the owner later wants email codes or email recovery; the template and SMTP tasks would return to M0. |
| D-37 (OD-18) | Superseded by D-67: V2 and V3 are specified in v8; M5-01 and M6-01 review them. | M5-01, M6-01 | V2 and V3 are defined again at M5-01 and M6-01. |
| D-38 (OD-19) | Members insert and remove only their own reactions; media rows only for messages they sent; any signed-in user may insert a chat with created_by = the caller; the creator manages chat_members; a member updates only last_read_seq, archived and muted_until on their own row; profiles readable by their owner and by members of a shared chat (D-42), writable by their owner. | M1-02 | B: the owner defines each policy table by table; the policy table in 10.5 is replaced. |
| D-39 (OD-20) | media_id on the client; path from chat_id and media_id; "already exists" counts as success. | M1-01, M1-06, M3-01, M3-02 | B: server-side de-duplication needs a new column and a function; the upload retry logic changes. |
| D-40 (OD-21) | Server-assigned per-chat seq and sync_seq from the chats.next_seq counter; the resync cursor is the highest sync_seq per chat. | M1-01, M1-03, M2-05 | Server timestamps with an inclusive cursor reintroduce rows missed behind the cursor (PR-6) and need an overlap window plus merge by key. |
| D-41 | Scale target 2 to 10 daily users; ceiling is the Free plan limits. | M4-05 | Higher scale needs the Pro plan. |
| D-42 | Profiles visible to self and shared-chat members; exact lookup through find_user. | M1-02, M1-03, M2-07 | An open profile read lets any user list every account. |
| D-43 | One connection per client; typing channel only for the open chat; delivery behind an interface. | M2-05, M2-11 | Switching to database Broadcast changes only the interface. |

10.3 Phased milestones
M1 to M4 implement the MVP phase of 3.1, M5 implements V2 and M6 implements V3. A milestone MUST NOT start before its entry condition holds, and it is complete only when its exit tests pass on the environments in 10.7.
| ID | Phase | Scope | Entry condition | Exit condition (tests) | Depends on |
| M0 | Pre-MVP | Accounts, tools, repository, hosted project, Auth, Realtime settings, Expo and Vercel setup (M0-01 to M0-10). | Sections 1 to 9 accepted by the owner. Region fixed by D-48. | Every M0 acceptance criterion passes. | None |
| M1 | MVP | Backend: schema, RLS, triggers, publication, Realtime authorization, Storage, database tests, test accounts (M1-01 to M1-09). | Local stack runs (M0-04). Hosted steps need M0-03, M0-05 to M0-07. | T-05, T-10, T-11, T-14, T-15, T-16, T-22 pass. | M0 |
| M2 | MVP | Shared layer, queue core, sign-in, sync, Markdown, chats, F1, F2, F3 (text), F4, F6, F7 (M2-01 to M2-13). | M1-01 to M1-05 and M1-08 done; M1-07 passes for those tasks. | T-01, T-02, T-04, T-06, T-09, T-12, T-13, T-17, T-18, T-19, T-37, T-38, T-51 pass. | M1 |
| M3 | MVP | Attachments, F5, forwarding notice for attachments, Low Signal Mode (F8), rate limits (M3-01 to M3-09). | M2 exit; T-11 fully passed (D-29 gives the lifetime). | T-03, T-07, T-08, T-20, T-21, T-39, T-41, T-52 pass; T-01, T-02, T-09 still pass. | M2 |
| M4 | MVP | F9, themes, cross-cutting states, latency measurement, runbook, scope review, admin web, MVP gate (M4-01 to M4-08). | M3 exit. | T-01 to T-22 and T-36 to T-53 all pass on the hosted project with release-candidate builds (3.1). | M3 |

10.4 Work breakdown
Tasks are listed in the order they MUST be started within each milestone. "Satisfies" names the requirements in the catalog of 10.10. A test ID in the acceptance cell is the matching test of 7.2 or 7.4; "none in 7.2" or "derived check" means neither has a matching test (PF-21).

M1 MVP backend foundation
| ID | Task and requirements | Deliverables | Acceptance criteria and test | Depends on / blockers |
| M1-01 | Core schema migration. Create the MVP tables of 6.2 plus the tables and columns decided in D-22, D-23, D-24, D-32, D-39 and D-40. IDs are uuid; chat_id and media_id are client-generated like message_id (D-10, D-39); timestamps are timestamptz. Every length CHECK counts Unicode code points and trims per D-116. / Satisfies: 6.2 profiles; 6.2 chats; 6.2 chat_members; 6.2 messages; 6.2 message_media; 6.2 reactions; 3.2 Chat; 3.2 Reply; 3.2 Read receipts; 3.2 Folders/archive/mute; LS-8; 4 Storage | Migration supabase/migrations/<ts>_core_schema.sql: / profiles(user_id, username unique with CHECK ^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{4,31}$, display_name with CHECK 1 to 64 characters after trimming (D-89), avatar_url, premium_flags); status omitted (D-32). / chats(chat_id, type direct|group (CHECK; secret added in V2; D-91), title (CHECK: 1 to 64 characters for a group, null otherwise; D-109), created_by, created_at, next_seq bigint default 0, direct_key text unique, null for groups (D-70)). / chat_members(chat_id, user_id, role owner|admin|member, joined_at, last_read_seq bigint default 0, archived boolean, muted_until timestamptz); PK (chat_id, user_id). / folders(folder_id, user_id, name with CHECK 1 to 32 characters) and folder_chats(folder_id, chat_id) with ON DELETE CASCADE from folders (D-24, D-94). / messages(message_id PK, chat_id, sender_id, content default '', reply_to, attachment_count smallint 0 to 10 (D-90), seq, sync_seq, edited_at, deleted_at, created_at); unique (chat_id, seq). / message_media(media_id PK, message_id, chat_id, storage_path, media_type, compressed, sync_seq). / reactions(message_id, chat_id, user_id, emoji, removed_at, sync_seq); PK (message_id, user_id, emoji). emoji CHECK in the D-22 palette (D-76). / media_type values photo|video|voice|file. Indexes on messages(chat_id, seq), on (chat_id, sync_seq) for messages, message_media and reactions, on chat_members(user_id, chat_id) and on every foreign key (D-44). The rate_limits table is added in M3-08. | The migration applies cleanly on the local stack and the hosted project; a schema dump lists exactly these tables and columns; inserting a second row with an existing message_id fails with unique violation 23505; inserting a chat with type channel fails the CHECK. / Test: precondition for T-09, T-16. | M0-04; M0-03 for hosted. |
| M1-02 | RLS: helper functions, enablement, policies, column grants. Enable RLS on every table and create the policies in 10.5. Membership and creator checks use helper functions in a non-exposed schema (SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE, pinned search_path, no user-id argument, EXECUTE revoked from anon) so policies on chat_members do not recurse (Supabase RLS documentation). Client write access is limited by column privileges as listed in 10.5. Policies call (select auth.uid()) so Postgres evaluates it once per statement (D-44). Every table policy, the Storage policies and the Realtime authorization also require is_active_user(), which is false while auth.users carries app_metadata.must_change_password, the account is banned or it is listed in private.disabled_users (D-78, D-79, D-86). The chat_members policies use role owner, not chats.created_by (D-70), and insert by the two branches of D-124. The helper reads auth.users and private.disabled_users, not the session token (D-86). profiles has no client UPDATE grant in the MVP, and premium_flags is readable (D-87, D-89). / Satisfies: 5.2 RLS enabled; 5.2 SELECT; 5.2 INSERT messages; 5.2 UPDATE messages; 5.2 Unlisted operations; 7.1 RLS exposure; 3.5 Security; 3.2 Edit/delete; 3.2 Reactions | Migration <ts>_rls.sql: helpers, ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY on all public tables, one policy per table and operation, GRANT/REVOKE statements; any operation without a policy stays denied. | Every allowed operation in 10.5 succeeds for the right test user; every other operation is denied for user C (non-member) and for user B where 10.5 says so; the catalog query lists every public table with RLS on. / Test: T-05, T-15, T-16. | M1-01. |
| M1-03 | Triggers: profile, Saved Messages, sequence numbers and timestamps, message update rule, reaction limit. Add the server-side triggers the decisions need: on auth.users insert create the profile and the Saved Messages chat (F2, D-70); assign seq, sync_seq, created_at, edited_at and deleted_at on the server (D-40); normalise message updates (D-22). / Satisfies: F2; F7; 3.2 Saved Messages; 6.2 messages; 3.2 Edit/delete; F4; 6.4 Resync | Migration <ts>_triggers.sql: / AFTER INSERT on auth.users: profiles row (username, display_name from user metadata; creation fails if username is missing), a direct chat with created_by = the user and direct_key = the user id, and one chat_members row (role owner). / BEFORE INSERT on messages: take the next value of chats.next_seq with UPDATE ... RETURNING (the chat row stays locked until commit), set seq and sync_seq to it, and set created_at = now(). / BEFORE UPDATE on messages: a content or deleted_at change takes the next counter value as sync_seq; a content change sets edited_at = now(); a delete (deleted_at set) sets deleted_at = now() and content = ''; any update when OLD.deleted_at is set is rejected, so a deleted message cannot be edited or restored; an update that changes neither content nor deleted_at is rejected; edited_at is not writable by clients (D-76). The same triggers reject blank text when attachment_count is 0, a reply_to in another chat, content over 4096 characters (over 22,000 base64 characters in a secret chat) and any client update after expires_at; only private.expire_messages() may update after expiry (D-90, D-111). / BEFORE INSERT or UPDATE on message_media and reactions: chat_id copied from the parent message and sync_seq taken from the next counter value; on message_media, storage_path set to {chat_id}/{media_id}, the attachment_count limit applied, and an object required at that path in the bucket of the media_type with owner_id = the caller and a declared type matching media_type (D-90); a reaction insert, or an update that clears removed_at, is rejected when the user already has 3 active reactions on that message (D-22, D-76). chat_members.last_read_seq never decreases and never exceeds chats.next_seq. / Function find_user(username text), SECURITY DEFINER with a pinned search_path: returns user_id and display_name for an exact, case-insensitive username match and nothing for a partial one; EXECUTE for role authenticated only (D-42). Its rate limit is added in M3-08. / BEFORE INSERT on chat_members: reject the row when the chat already has 100 members (D-58). The same trigger allows at most two rows in a direct chat and one in Saved Messages, sets chats.direct_key when the second member of a direct chat is added, and rejects a group member who shares no chat with the member adding them (D-70). AFTER DELETE on chat_members: when the owner leaves a group, the longest-standing remaining member becomes owner: earliest joined_at, then lowest user_id (D-70, D-91). BEFORE UPDATE on auth.users: when encrypted_password changes, remove must_change_password from raw_app_meta_data (D-78; Assumption: Supabase permits this trigger as it does the insert trigger; fallback: the initial-password check of D-86). | Creating a user through the Admin API creates one profile and one Saved Messages chat with one member row; as that user the chat list returns exactly that chat. A content update sets edited_at; a delete sets deleted_at and empties content; editing a deleted message is rejected; a client-supplied created_at, seq or sync_seq is rejected by column privileges; two concurrent inserts into one chat receive distinct, increasing seq values in commit order; a fourth active reaction by one user on one message is rejected. Clearing removed_at on a fourth reaction is rejected; a third direct-chat member and a duplicate direct chat for the same pair are rejected; a new password clears must_change_password. Blank text with attachment_count 0, a reply_to into another chat and a 4097-character content are rejected; the owner leaving passes ownership by the D-91 tie-break. / Test: T-06 (API half), T-38, T-39. | M1-01, M1-02. |
| M1-04 | Realtime publication. Add the three tables 6.4 names to the supabase_realtime publication; add no others until M5-03 adds polls and poll_votes (D-67). Re-read the Supabase Realtime limits, pricing and RLS-performance pages and correct 6.4 and 7.1 (D-85). / Satisfies: 6.4 Publication; 4 Realtime; 6.1 Realtime sync; 3.2 Chat | Migration <ts>_realtime.sql: ALTER PUBLICATION supabase_realtime ADD TABLE messages, message_media, reactions. | A catalog query on pg_publication_tables returns exactly messages, message_media and reactions before V2; hard DELETE has no grant on any of them, so no unfiltered DELETE event exists (D-14). / Test: precondition for T-01, T-13, T-17. | M1-01. |
| M1-05 | Realtime Authorization for typing channels. Authorize per-chat Broadcast channels with policies on realtime.messages; Presence is refused (D-118). Topic names chat:<chat_id> and, in V3, call:<chat_id> and user:<user_id> (D-98, D-100); channels are opened with private: true. / Satisfies: 6.4 Channels; 3.2 Typing; 4 Realtime; 7.1 RLS exposure | Migration <ts>_realtime_auth.sql: SELECT and INSERT policies for role authenticated, extension broadcast only (D-118), allowed only when the user is an active member of the chat parsed from realtime.topic() (D-86); V3 adds the user:<user_id> rule of D-98. | A member joins chat:<id> and exchanges a broadcast; a non-member is refused or receives nothing; a public channel is refused; a Presence join is refused (D-118); a read hint sent by one member reaches the other (D-92). / Test: T-14. | M1-02, M0-07. |
| M1-06 | Storage bucket and policies. Create the two private media buckets of D-75 and restrict upload to chat members and download to chat members while the object's message is live. Object path is <chat_id>/<media_id> (D-39; no extension). No UPDATE or DELETE policy exists, so upsert and overwrite are refused. Bucket size and MIME limits come from D-29 and D-75, set in bytes (D-116), and the signed URL lifetime (10 minutes) from D-113. / Satisfies: 5.3 Bucket policies; 5.3 Signed URLs; 6.1 Media storage; 3.2 Media; 5.5 File validation; F5 | Migration <ts>_storage.sql: buckets chat-media-small (10 MB; the image and audio types of 5.5) and chat-media-large (50 MB; every type of 5.5), both public false (D-75); an INSERT policy on storage.objects checking membership of the chat in the first path segment (malformed segment = denied); a SELECT policy that also requires a message_media row for the object whose message is neither deleted nor expired. | A member uploads to <own chat>/<new uuid>; a second upload to the same path fails with "already exists"; an upload under another chat's prefix is denied; a non-member cannot download or create a signed URL; a signed URL stops working after the D-113 lifetime; a 12 MB photo is refused by chat-media-small; after the sender deletes the message, a member's download and signed-URL request are denied (D-75). / Test: T-11. | M1-02. |
| M1-07 | Database and API tests. Write pgTAP tests for every policy, trigger, grant and the publication, and an API test script that signs in as users A, B and C and runs T-05, T-15 and T-16. pgTAP tests supplement the API tests; they do not replace them (D-16). / Satisfies: 7.3 RLS via API; 7.1 RLS exposure; 5.2 RLS enabled | supabase/tests/*.sql; packages/shared/tests/api/* (Vitest, D-100). | supabase test db passes locally; the API script passes locally and on hosted. Mutation check: dropping any one policy makes at least one test fail. A denied UPDATE is asserted on affected rows, not on an absent error. / Test: T-05, T-15, T-16. | M1-02 to M1-06. |
| M1-08 | Account creation procedure, test accounts, fixtures. Document how the owner creates accounts (D-47: sign-up disabled; the Admin API with the email confirmed, a first password of at least 12 characters, and username and display_name in user metadata; the secret key is used only from a local script). The owner gives each contact their first password out-of-band and resets forgotten passwords the same way. New accounts carry app_metadata.must_change_password = true, which users cannot edit; until a password change clears it, every policy denies the account (D-78). After a reset the script sets the new password first and the flag second. scripts/disable-user bans an account and keeps its messages (D-61); if the ban does not set banned_until, it inserts the user into private.disabled_users (D-86). Both scripts validate username and display_name against D-89. Create test accounts A, B, C and G (10.2) and fixture chats; G joins the fixture group A+B+G and the V2 fixture group (D-126). From M4-08 the scripts call packages/admin-core, which the admin web also uses, and every script action writes private.admin_audit (D-102, D-106). / Satisfies: 5.1 Password sign-in; F1 | docs/accounts.md; scripts scripts/create-user (one account, or a CSV file of accounts, D-81) and scripts/disable-user; fixture script creating a direct chat A-B, a group A+B and a group A+B+G (C a member of none) through the API under RLS. | Four users exist; each has a profile and a Saved Messages chat; the fixture chats exist; user C is a member of no chat with A or B; create-user refuses a username of 4 characters (D-89). / Test: setup for T-05, T-10, T-11, T-14, T-15, T-22. | M1-03. |

| M2-02 | Local persistence adapters. Implement one LocalStore interface with an expo-sqlite adapter (mobile) and an IndexedDB adapter (web).
