| M2-03 | Queue core and delivery worker (text). Implement the queue state machine and worker for text items. The message rows of one chat are inserted strictly in enqueue order: an item whose row insert is backing off or failed blocks later items of the same chat until it is sent or discarded, other chats are not blocked, and attachment uploads never block (D-27, D-93). Fault-injectable fetch lets tests drop a response after the server stored the row. / Satisfies: LS-1; LS-2; LS-3; LS-4; LS-7; LS-8; LS-9; LS-10; 6.3 Queue worker; 6.4 Insert flow; 7.1 Duplicate sends; 7.1 Signal loss UX | State machine queued -> sending -> sent | failed; enqueue writes the item before any network call with temp_id = message_id; worker triggers on connectivity change (mobile: netinfo; web: browser online event) and on a timer whenever any connection is reported (interval from D-27), only while the app or tab is in the foreground (D-109, D-117); a rejected insert with 23505 marks the item sent; backoff and attempt cap from D-27, with wake-up and attempt counting per D-77; sending items return to queued at startup; failed items stay visible with Retry (retry_count reset to 0) and Discard (a soft delete first when the row exists, disabled offline) (D-93); enqueue refuses a 201st unsent item with a visible message (D-27, D-93). | Fake-clock, fake-network unit tests cover every transition. A test that drops the response after the insert leaves exactly one row. Kill-and-reopen with a persisted store loses no item. The 201st unsent item is refused. Retry of a failed item resets its count; Discard of an item whose row exists soft-deletes it. / Test: T-09 (automated), T-41; precondition for T-02, T-18, T-19. | M2-02. Constants come from the config module (D-27); tests inject values. |
| M2-04 | F1 sign-in and session (both clients). Build email and password sign-in, sign-out and change password. Mobile keeps the session in AsyncStorage and starts and stops token auto-refresh with app foreground state (Supabase Expo guide; Assumption checked here, fallback a session refresh on every foreground, D-85); web uses the SDK's default browser storage. / Satisfies: F1; 5.1 Password sign-in; 5.1 JWT; 4 Transport; 5.4 HTTPS/WSS | Email and password screen; signInWithPassword; a forced Change password screen before the chat list when app_metadata.must_change_password is true; after updateUser succeeds the client refreshes the session so the new JWT lacks the flag (D-78); access does not wait for the refresh, because is_active_user() reads auth.users (D-86); email syntax check; 12-character minimum on a new password; offline error; session restore on start; settings entries Sign out and Change password (updateUser). | An unknown email or a wrong password creates no session; a correct password opens the chat list (after the forced password change for a new account); the session survives app restart and page refresh; offline sign-in shows an error and no session; wording per D-35. Sign out ends the session and returns to the sign-in screen; Change password works while signed in and the old password stops working. / Test: T-10 (re-run through the UI on both clients). | M2-01, M0-05, M1-08. |
| M2-05 | Sync module. Subscribe to Postgres Changes on messages, message_media and reactions with no client filter (RLS filters, 6.4). Delivery sits behind a small interface in packages/shared so it can be replaced (D-43). Resync on foreground and reconnect, per chat, with the cursor equal to the highest sync_seq received for that chat (D-40); merge by key. / Satisfies: 6.4 Resync; 7.1 Channel drops; 3.2 Chat; F3; 4 Realtime; 6.1 Realtime sync | Event handlers writing to the local cache; initial fetch; resync fetch, in pages of 500 rows, of rows of messages, message_media and reactions with sync_seq > the chat's cursor; fetch of an unknown chat and its members when an event names one; read state and folder settings refreshed on open and foreground (not in the publication); a chat missing from the chat list fetch has its local cache and sync state deleted (D-91). | A second device sees the same history; replaying one event twice leaves the cache unchanged; a test with a skewed device clock shows the cursor never uses the device clock; after backgrounding, missed messages, edits, media rows and reactions appear, and rows written by two concurrent senders all arrive; after removal from a group, the chat and its cache are gone on the next foreground (D-91). / Test: T-13, T-38; precondition for T-01, T-04. | M2-02, M1-04. |
| M2-06 | Markdown renderer (both clients). Render message text as Markdown only. Mobile: react-native-markdown-display; web: react-markdown. Do not enable raw HTML. Syntax limited to D-30 (bold, italic, code, links); headings, lists, block quotes, tables and rules render as the plain text of their children and an image as its alt text (D-99); remote images are never loaded; links allow http, https, mailto, tel. Do this task first in M2: the mobile library's Expo SDK compatibility is untested (3.2 Assumption). / Satisfies: 3.2 Formatting; 5.5 Markdown safety; F3 | Shared message-text component per client. | Input containing <script>alert(1)</script>, <b>x</b>, an unclosed ** and unbalanced brackets renders literally on both clients, throws no error and runs nothing; a heading renders as plain text and an image link makes no request (D-99). | M2-07 | Find users, create chats, manage members. A removes B from a group and an API read as B then returns no rows for that chat; a non-owner's add or remove of another member is rejected; a member who leaves loses read access; adding a user who shares no chat with the owner is rejected; members of a direct chat cannot be removed; adding a 101st member is rejected; when the owner leaves, the earliest-joined member becomes owner (D-91); an unknown username shows the not-found message (D-35). find_user returns one row for an exact username and nothing for a partial one, so users cannot list each other (T-22). / Test: T-38; T-05 and T-15 re-run. | M2-05, M1-02. |
| M2-08 | F2 chat list. Build the chat list from the local cache, newest message first by messages.created_at. | M2-10 | F4 edit and delete. Edit own messages by UPDATE of content; delete by UPDATE of deleted_at (the trigger clears the content). | M2-11 | F6 typing indicators and read receipts. Read state: set chat_members.last_read_seq to the seq of the newest message displayed (D-23); a pending update is applied on reconnection. After writing last_read_seq the client sends a read hint on chat:<id>, and members with the chat open re-read chat_members through RLS (D-92). A message is shown as seen when another member's last_read_seq is at or above its seq: the other member in a direct chat, at least one other member in a group (D-92). | M3-07 | Delivery policy and photo compression. Apply the LS-5 matrix at each attempt from the network state at that moment (D-117): text immediately; photos compressed with expo-image-manipulator in Automatic and Forced to JPEG at quality 0.7, long edge at most 1280 px and never enlarged, with the compressed file kept for later attempts (message_media.compressed = true, D-117); video, voice notes and files wait for Wi-Fi when Forced, and when Automatic only above the large-media threshold; No connection keeps everything queued; a deferred item is sent as soon as Wi-Fi is reported, immediately if already on Wi-Fi. Videos are never transcoded (D-07). Web does none of this (D-19). / Satisfies: LS-5; 6.3 Compression; F8; 7.1 Large uploads; 3.2 Low Signal Mode; 3.4 Triggers | Policy engine, compression step, Wi-Fi wait handling. | On a simulated 2G/3G cellular connection a photo is compressed, shows "sending (low signal)" and its row has compressed true; with the toggle on, new photos are compressed and new photos, videos, voice notes and files show "waiting for Wi-Fi" until Wi-Fi connects; a video queued before a text in the same chat does not delay the text, and the video's message keeps the lower seq (D-93). / Test: T-03, T-08, T-41. | M3-05, M3-06. Values: threshold 5 MB; compression 1280 px long edge at quality 0.7 (D-28). |
| M3-08 | Rate limits. Add the rate_limits table, keyed by user and action, and take one token per action: a BEFORE INSERT trigger on messages (burst 200, refill 1 token per second, so 60 per minute; D-29), a check inside find_user (30 tokens, refill 1 per 2 seconds) and a BEFORE INSERT trigger on chats (20 tokens, refill 1 per 72 minutes; D-46, D-122). The burst equals the maximum queue length (D-27), so a full offline queue can be flushed. A rejection raises SQLSTATE DG429 with the retry time in seconds in the error detail; the client keeps the item queued until then and does not count it as an attempt (D-77). / Satisfies: 5.5 Rate limit | Migration <ts>_rate_limit.sql (table and trigger, no client privileges); client handling of the rejection. | A burst of 200 messages succeeds; messages beyond the refill rate are rejected through the API and create no row; rejected items retry under LS-7. The 31st lookup in a minute and the 21st chat created in a day are rejected. / Test: T-21. | M2-03, M1-03. |

| M5-10 | F11 push notifications (Android). Register tokens with Expo Notifications, store them in push_tokens with own-row RLS (D-25, D-100), send with a database webhook that calls an Edge Function that calls the Expo Push API. The payload shows the chat name and "New message" and carries only chat_id as data (D-49). The Edge Function accepts only calls that carry the PUSH_WEBHOOK_SECRET bearer token and sends to members other than the sender who are active, not muted (D-09) and have a token; the title is the group title or the sender's display name (D-96). The app registers a token only after the notification permission is granted. Upload FCM credentials to EAS (D-11). / Satisfies: F11; 3.2 Push; 6.1 Push | Token table, policies, Edge Function, webhook, EAS credentials, permission handling. | With the app in the background a new message raises a notification on the Android build; the payload holds no message text; a muted chat raises none; with the OS permission denied no alert appears and the app otherwise works; a webhook call without the secret gets 401 (D-96). / Test: T-23. | M5-01. Expo's push service has no charge (Expo push FAQ, checked 26 October 2026); FCM credentials come from a Firebase project on the free plan (Assumption: no billing account is needed; checked here, fallback: push is not built and T-23 is recorded N/A, D-85, D-121). |
| M5-12 | Forward attachments. Copy each attachment object to the target chat's prefix with a new media_id through the Storage copy call, then insert the message_media row, so the forwarded message no longer depends on the original (D-50). Copies count against the 1 GB storage limit and the monthly check (D-45). / Satisfies: 3.2 Forward; F3 | Copy step in the forward flow; the M3-04 notice removed. | A forwarded photo has its own object and message_media row and stays readable after the original is deleted. / Test: T-32. | M5-01, M3-04. |
| M5-13 | Profile editing and avatars. Settings gains Edit profile (display name, username, avatar); this task adds the profiles UPDATE grant of D-89, with the D-89 formats. Avatars upload to the avatars bucket and profiles.avatar_url stores the path; clients read it through a signed URL (D-51, D-72). / Satisfies: 6.2 profiles; 5.2 Profile privacy | Avatars bucket migration and policies; profile screen on both clients. | The owner edits their own profile; another user cannot; an oversize or wrong-type avatar is refused. / Test: T-33. | M5-01, M1-02. |
| M5-11 | V2 gate. Run T-23 to T-33 (7.4), re-run T-01 to T-22 and T-36 to T-53 and the RLS tests for every new table. A test recorded N/A under a D-85 fallback counts as passed (D-121). / Satisfies: 3.1 V3 gate; 3.5 Security | Test log docs/test-results/V2.md. | T-01 to T-33 and T-36 to T-53 pass. / Test: T-01 to T-33, T-36 to T-53. | M5-02 to M5-10, M5-12, M5-13. |
---TABLE END---

10.5 Backend build order
| Step | Task | Why in this order | Verify |
| 1 | M0-04 | Everything else is written and tested against the local stack first. | supabase start runs; CLI linked. |
| 2 | M1-01 | Tables and columns must exist before policies, triggers or publication refer to them. | Schema dump matches M1-01; duplicate message_id fails. |
| 3 | M1-02 | RLS and column grants come before any data or client exists, so no table is ever readable without a policy. | T-05, T-15, T-16 against the local stack. |
| 4 | M1-03 | Triggers depend on the tables and on the grants of step 3. | Profile and Saved Messages created; update rules hold. |
| 5 | M1-04 | The publication is added after policies, so events are RLS-filtered from the start. | Publication lists exactly three tables. |
| 6 | M1-05 | Reuses the membership helper from step 3. | T-14. |
| 7 | M1-06 | Reuses the membership helper; uses the D-29, D-113 and D-116 values for limits and lifetime. | T-11. |
| 8 | M0-05, M0-06, M0-07 | Auth, password policy and Realtime settings: local first, hosted once M0-03 exists. | T-10 and T-14 settings checks. |
| 9 | M1-07 | Database tests are written with steps 3 to 7 and run in full here. | supabase test db and API tests pass; mutation check. |
| 10 | M1-08 | Needs the trigger of step 4 and working Auth. | Accounts and fixtures exist. |
| 11 | M1-09 | Run the M1 test set on local and hosted. | T-05, T-10, T-11, T-14, T-15, T-16, T-22. |
| 12 | M3-08 | Rate limit comes last because it needs the queue size (D-27) and the message insert path of M2-03. | T-21 (D-122). |
| 13 | M5-01 to M5-13 (V2 migrations) | V2 tables, columns, policies and the push webhook build on the MVP schema and start after the MVP gate (D-67). | T-23 to T-33, and T-05, T-15 and T-16 for each new table. |
| 14 | M6-01, M6-02 (V3 Realtime authorization) | The call:<chat_id> and user:<user_id> rules (D-73, D-98) reuse the membership helper. | T-34. |
---TABLE END---

Policy table (used by M1-02 to M1-06)
Every operation not listed is denied: with RLS enabled an operation without a policy is denied (5.2). Writable-column limits are enforced with column privileges, and server-assigned sequence numbers and timestamps with triggers (D-40).
| Table | Operation | Rule | Source |
| profiles | SELECT | Rows of the caller and of users who share a chat with the caller; other users are found only through find_user (D-42). Every column is readable, premium_flags included (D-87). | D-38 |
| find_user (function) | EXECUTE | Role authenticated only. Returns user_id and display_name for an exact, case-insensitive username; limited to 30 calls per minute per user (refill rate: D-122). | D-42; D-46 |
| profiles | INSERT / DELETE | No client policy; the trigger creates the row. | 5.2; M1-03 |
| profiles | UPDATE | V2 (M5-13) only: own row; client may write username, display_name, avatar_url (premium_flags excluded, D-32). No client update in the MVP (D-89). | D-38; D-32 |
| chats | SELECT | Rows of chats where the user has a chat_members row. | 5.2 |
| chats | INSERT | created_by = the caller; type direct or group, and secret from V2, by CHECK (D-91); at most 20 chats per user per day (D-38, D-46, D-122). | D-26 |
| chats | UPDATE / DELETE | None (denied). | D-38 default |
| chat_members | SELECT | Rows of chats where the user has a chat_members row. | 5.2 |
| chat_members | INSERT | Two branches (D-124): the creator's own first row (user_id = the caller, role owner, chats.created_by = the caller, no members yet); any other row is added by the chat owner with role member, up to 100 per chat (D-58). A new group member must already share a chat with the member who adds them; a direct chat holds exactly two rows, with no shared-chat rule, and Saved Messages one (D-70, D-124); a secret chat holds two rows whose users already share a direct chat (V2, D-91). | D-26; D-38 |
| chat_members | UPDATE | Own row; columns last_read_seq, archived, muted_until only (D-38). | D-23; D-24 |
| chat_members | DELETE | Groups only: the owner removes a member, and any member deletes their own row to leave; when the owner leaves, the longest-standing member becomes owner. Rows of direct and Saved Messages chats cannot be deleted (D-70). | D-26 |
| messages | SELECT | Members of the chat. | 5.2 |
| messages | INSERT | sender_id = the caller and the caller is a member. Writable columns: message_id, chat_id, sender_id, content, reply_to, attachment_count (kind, payload and ttl_seconds from V2); seq, sync_seq and created_at are not writable. Triggers apply the checks of D-90. | 5.2; D-40 |
| messages | UPDATE | The sender only. Writable columns: content and deleted_at (a delete sets any non-null value); edited_at is not writable. Triggers set edited_at and deleted_at to now(), clear content on delete and reject an update that changes neither column (D-76). After expires_at only private.expire_messages() may update (D-111). | 5.2; D-22 |
| messages | DELETE | None; messages are soft-deleted (D-14). | D-14 |
| message_media | SELECT | Members of the message's chat, only while the parent message is neither deleted nor expired (D-75, D-69). | 5.2 |
| message_media | INSERT | Only for a message the caller sent, up to its attachment_count, for an object at {chat_id}/{media_id} that the caller uploaded to the bucket of its media_type with a matching declared type (D-90). | D-38 |
| message_media | UPDATE / DELETE | None (denied). | D-38 default |
| reactions | SELECT | Members of the message's chat. | 5.2 |
| reactions | INSERT | user_id = the caller and the caller is a member of the message's chat; emoji in the palette (CHECK); at most 3 active reactions per user per message (trigger, D-76). | D-38 |
| reactions | UPDATE | Own rows; column removed_at only (soft removal). Clearing removed_at re-adds the reaction under the same 3-reaction limit (D-76). | D-22 |
| reactions | DELETE | None; a hard DELETE event would reach every subscriber (6.4). | 6.4 |
| folders, folder_chats | All | Own rows only (folder owned by the caller); a folder_chats row also requires the caller to be a member of the chat (D-24, D-84). At most 10 folders per user (trigger) and names of 1 to 32 characters (CHECK) (D-94). | F9 |
| rate_limits | All | None for clients; the triggers on messages and chats and the find_user function use it (D-46). | D-29 |
| private.admin_audit, private.disabled_users, private.initial_passwords | All | None for clients: the private schema is not exposed, RLS is enabled with no policy, and the admin web and scripts write over SUPABASE_DB_URL (D-86, D-106). | D-106 |
| storage.objects (chat-media-small, chat-media-large) | SELECT, INSERT | INSERT: the caller is a member of the chat named by the first path segment. SELECT (download and signed URL): also a message_media row for the object exists and its message is neither deleted nor expired (D-75, D-69); signed URLs last 10 minutes (D-113). | 5.3 |
| storage.objects (chat-media-small, chat-media-large) | UPDATE, DELETE | None; upsert and overwrite are refused. | D-39 |
| realtime.messages | SELECT, INSERT | Extension broadcast only; Presence is refused (D-118). Topic chat:<chat_id> (typing and read hints, D-92) or call:<chat_id> (V3 signalling, D-73), caller is an active member of that chat (D-79, D-86). Topic user:<user_id> (V3 invitations): SELECT by that user only; INSERT by an active member of a direct chat shared with that user (D-98). Authorization is re-checked on each join and each new JWT (D-114). | 6.4 |
| chat_members (V2) | UPDATE role, restricted_until | The owner sets role (admin or member) on other members' rows; the owner or an admin sets restricted_until on a member's row; nobody restricts the owner, and an admin does not restrict another admin. | D-71 |
| messages, reactions, poll_votes (V2) | INSERT, UPDATE | Also require that the caller is not restricted in the chat, except an UPDATE that only sets deleted_at (messages) or removed_at (reactions, poll_votes) (D-112); in a secret chat, reactions and media rows are refused. A sticker with a p_ id requires premium_stickers (D-97). Payloads per D-120; extra emoji per D-119. | D-68; D-71; D-97; D-112 |
| polls (V2) | SELECT / INSERT / UPDATE | Members read; the sender inserts it only through send_poll with its message, and a poll message without its poll fails at commit (D-120); the sender sets closed_at only. No DELETE. | D-67 |
| poll_votes (V2) | SELECT / INSERT / UPDATE | Members read; a member inserts their own vote while the poll is open, with option_index inside the options (D-120); UPDATE of option_index and removed_at on their own row only. No DELETE. | D-67 |
| secret_keys (V2) | SELECT / INSERT | Members of the secret chat read; a member inserts their own row once. No UPDATE or DELETE. | D-68 |
| push_tokens (V2) | All | Own rows only. | D-25 |
| push Edge Function (V2) | Webhook call | Requires the header Authorization: Bearer <PUSH_WEBHOOK_SECRET>; any other caller gets 401 and nothing is sent. | D-96 |
| storage.objects (avatars, V2) | SELECT / INSERT / DELETE | Read: the caller may read the owner's profile. Write and delete: the caller's own {user_id} folder; at most 512 KB of JPEG, PNG or WebP. | D-72 |
---TABLE END---

10.6 Client build order
| Step | Layer | Tasks | Note |
| 1 | Shared | M2-01 | Data access, validation, ids, config, strings. Everything else imports it. |
| 2 | Shared | M2-02 | Local store interface with the SQLite and IndexedDB adapters. |
| 3 | Shared | M2-03 | Queue core and worker. LS-1 makes this come before any text can be sent. |
| 4 | Shared | M2-06 (spike) | Test the mobile Markdown library on the chosen SDK early; it is the one untested library. |
| 5 | Both | M2-04, M2-05 | Sign-in and sync. Mobile and web can proceed in parallel. |
| 6 | Both | M2-07, M2-08, M2-09, M2-10, M2-11, M2-12 | Flows in the order F1, F2, F3, F4, F6, F7. Mobile and web in parallel within each flow. |
| 7 | Both | M3-01 to M3-04 | Attachment pipeline, queue extension, F5 UI, forwarding notice for attachments (D-50). |
| 8 | Mobile | M3-05 to M3-07 | Low Signal Mode last: it builds on the queue and the attachment pipeline. Web has none (D-19). |
| 9 | Both | M4-01 to M4-04 | F9, themes, cross-cutting states, latency measurement. |
| 10 | Admin | M4-08 | Local admin web; starts once M1-08 and M4-05 exist and finishes before the MVP gate M4-07 (D-102). |
| 11 | Mobile | M5-09, M5-10 | App lock and push (V2). |
| 12 | Both | M5-02 to M5-08, M5-12, M5-13 | The other V2 features, after the V2 review (M5-01); Secret Chats are mobile-only (D-33). |
| 13 | Both | M6-02, M6-03 | Calls (V3), after the M6-01 spike; web-only if it fails (D-98, D-121). |
---TABLE END---

10.7 Testing plan
Every test of 7.2 first runs in the milestone shown. RLS, Storage and Realtime tests run through the API with each test user's own session, never in the SQL Editor (D-16); pgTAP tests supplement them. T-01 to T-22 and T-36 to T-53 run again at M4-07, M5-11 and after every change to a policy. A test recorded N/A under a D-85 fallback counts as passed (D-121). T-23 to T-33 of 7.4 first run in M5 and T-34 and T-35 in M6, on the hosted project. Environments: L local stack, H hosted, D device, W browser.

10.8 Deployment and release
Roll out in this order: backend, web, mobile, contacts. The development build installed for testing is the one 6.5 and D-11 describe; the build for daily use is a standalone build (PF-22). Neither goes to a store.
| Step | Action | Verify | Rollback |
| R-1 | Run the local test set (pgTAP and API tests) and take the D-29 backup export of the hosted database. | All green; dump file exists. | N/A |
| R-2 | Apply migrations to the hosted project with supabase db push. | supabase migration list shows local and remote in sync; T-16 on hosted. | Forward-fix with a new migration; restore the dump if data is damaged (Storage files are not in it, PR-3). |
| R-3 | Confirm hosted Auth and Realtime settings against the table in 10.2. | T-10 and T-14 settings checks. | Re-apply the settings. |
| R-4 | Run T-05, T-11, T-14, T-15, T-16, T-22, T-38, T-39 against the hosted project through each test user's session. | All pass. | Stop the release; fix policies by migration. |
| R-5 | Deploy the web app: preview deployment first, then production on Vercel Hobby (D-115); set the public variables. | T-01 against the deployment. | Redeploy the previous Vercel deployment. |
| R-6 | Build the Android app with EAS Build on the Free plan (eas build --platform android, using an APK profile). Development build for testing (D-11); a standalone internal-distribution build for daily use (PF-22). Install the APK directly; no store submission. | App installs; sign-in works; T-01 across mobile and web. | Reinstall the previous APK; keep it until the new build is accepted. |
| R-7 | Create the contacts' accounts in the admin web (M4-08, D-102), which follows the M1-08 procedure (D-47), and give each contact the APK, the web address and a first password out-of-band. | Each contact signs in and sees Saved Messages. | Delete the account in the admin web (D-110), not in the Dashboard, which skips the D-110 steps. |
| R-8 | Check usage and project status on the admin web Overview (D-107), or on the Supabase dashboard if a panel is unavailable. | Usage under the 7.1 limits, or the D-123 action taken; project not paused. | Unpause from the Dashboard. |
---TABLE END---

10.9 Risks and sequencing notes
Risks from 7.1 and how the plan treats them
| Risk (7.1) | Plan treatment | Tasks |
| Free plan limits exceeded | Compression in M3-07; usage check and the D-123 actions in M4-05; typing throttle in M2-11 (PR-8). | M3-07, M4-05 |
| Free project paused after 1 week | Runbook step to unpause; R-8. | M4-05 |
| No automatic backups | D-29 manual export; restore drill; Storage gap (PR-3). | M4-05 |
| RLS misconfiguration exposes private chats | Policies in 10.5 with tests T-05, T-06, T-11, T-14, T-15, T-16, T-22, T-38, T-39, T-51, T-52; re-run at M4-07 and M5-11. | M1-02, M1-07, M4-07 |
| Offline queue creates duplicate sends | message_id primary key; 23505 counts as delivered; T-09. | M1-01, M2-03 |
| Poor UX when signal is lost mid-send | Status always visible; failed stays visible; T-18, T-41. | M2-03, M3-06 |
| Realtime channel drops on background/foreground | Resync by per-chat sync_seq cursor; T-13. | M2-05 |
| Large uploads fail on 2G/3G | LS-5 deferral and "waiting for Wi-Fi" label; T-08. Threshold 5 MB (D-28). | M3-07 |
| Secret Chat encryption implemented incorrectly | libsodium only; confirm functions; known vectors (V2). | M5-07 |
| Lost or leaked password | Owner-run reset; 12-character minimum; T-10. | M0-05, M0-06 |
| Inconsistent behavior between clients | One shared package for validation, ids, queue core and sync; same tests on both clients. | M0-02, M2-01 |
| Admin web exposes the secret key or private data | Local-only, passcode, Origin and Host checks, no content functions; T-42 to T-44. | M4-08 |
---TABLE END---

Risks the plan introduces
| ID | Risk | Severity | Mitigation | Tasks |
| PR-1 | Region cannot be changed after project creation. | High | The region is Tokyo (D-48, D-56) and is already fixed; no migration path is planned. | M0-03 |
| PR-2 | Password sign-in relies on the project minimum password length; nothing enforces the 12 characters D-47 requires until the project setting is raised (the Supabase password security documentation only recommends at least 8). | Medium | Set 12 explicitly on the hosted project and in config.toml; T-10 checks that a shorter password is refused. | M0-05, T-10 |
| PR-3 | Database exports do not contain Storage files (Supabase backup documentation); D-29 therefore does not protect media. | Medium | Monthly media copy with a supabase-js script through the Storage API (D-52, D-74); loss between copies is accepted. | M4-05 |
| PR-4 | SECURITY DEFINER helpers and column grants can be misconfigured and expose or over-permit. | Medium | Helpers in a non-exposed schema, no user-id argument, pinned search_path, EXECUTE revoked from anon; pgTAP and mutation check. | M1-02, M1-07 |
| PR-5 | A denied UPDATE can return zero rows without an error, so a test can pass on a write that never happened. | Medium | Tests ask for the changed rows back and assert the count and the unchanged data. | M1-07, T-06, T-15 |
| PR-6 | A long transaction on one chat holds the sequence counter lock and delays other writers in that chat. | Low | Message inserts are single statements and the lock is held only for the write; accepted at personal scale. | M1-03 |
| PR-7 | message_media and reactions need chat_id and sync_seq to take part in per-chat resync. | Medium | Both tables carry a trigger-set chat_id and sync_seq (D-40) and the resync fetches all three tables. | M1-01, M2-05 |
| PR-8 | Typing events count toward the 2 million Realtime messages per month in 7.1. | Medium | Typing sends one event per 3 seconds (D-57) and a read hint one event per read (D-92); check the usage dashboard. | M2-11, M4-05 |
| PR-9 | The device may not report cellular generation, so the Automatic trigger never fires. | Medium | Spike in M3-05; unknown is treated as not 2G/3G (D-13); the Forced toggle still works. The spike never gates T-03 (D-85). | M3-05, T-03 |
| PR-10 | react-native-markdown-display is untested on the chosen Expo SDK. | Medium | Spike first in M2-06; if it fails use react-native-marked (D-65) and record the result in the test log. | M2-06 |
| PR-11 | react-markdown may drop HTML tags instead of showing them. | Low | T-12; if the tags are dropped, add the HTML-node-to-text handler (D-85). | M2-06 |
| PR-12 | Picker file URIs may expire before a queued upload runs. | Medium | Copy attachments into app-private storage at enqueue; T-07. | M3-02 |
| PR-13 | Forwarding attachments copies objects and uses Storage quota. | Low | Resolved by D-50: attachment forwarding is not built in the MVP. | M3-04 |
| PR-14 | A development-client build cannot run without a development server or update URL (Expo development builds documentation). | Medium | Standalone internal-distribution build for daily use (PF-22). | M0-08, R-6 |
| PR-15 | The Free plan's 200 concurrent Realtime connections and 1 GB of storage are reached long before 50,000 accounts. | Medium | Monthly usage check that acts at 80% of any limit (D-45, D-123); media size caps (D-29); the Pro plan is the escape hatch. | M4-05 |
| PR-16 | Postgres Changes may not keep up with many simultaneous clients. | Low | Delivery sits behind an interface (D-43); database Broadcast replaces it at 160 peak connections (D-123). | M2-05 |
| PR-17 | A chat member can forge another member's typing indicator (D-92). | Low | Typing is advisory and stores nothing; read state is always re-read from the database. | M2-11 |
| PR-18 | The push webhook secret could leak and allow notification spam (D-96). | Low | The secret lives only in Edge Function secrets and the webhook settings; rotating it needs no app build. | M5-10 |
| PR-19 | A Management API endpoint the admin web uses may be missing for Free projects or may change (D-107). | Low | Each panel shows "unavailable" with a Dashboard link; usage can be entered by hand. | M4-08 |
| PR-20 | Deleting an account soft-deletes its messages, which the other members lose (D-108). | Medium | Only on request, after typing the username, with the effect stated on screen; deletion erases and disables without a cascading delete (D-110); T-53. | M4-08 |
---TABLE END---

Sequencing notes
Critical path: M0-03 -> M1-01 -> M1-02 -> M1-03 to M1-06 -> M1-08 -> M1-09 -> M2-03 -> M2-09 -> M3-02 -> M3-07 -> M4-08 -> M4-07.
Local work needs no hosted project: M0-01, M0-02, M0-04, M0-08, M0-09, all of M1 on the local stack, and M2-01 to M2-06 can proceed before M0-03 exists.
Configuration values are fixed (D-27, D-28, D-29, D-30, D-35, D-113, D-116, D-122) and read from one config module, so changing one needs no change outside that module.
Mobile and web build in parallel after M2-01 to M2-03; the shared package is the only shared dependency.
M2 can start before T-11 fully passes; M3 cannot, because the attachment pipeline relies on the Storage policies and the signed URL lifetime.
M5 and M6 begin with a review task (M5-01, M6-01) that confirms the V2 and V3 definitions of D-67 to D-73 against the MVP as built; nothing in them is estimated here.
10.10 Traceability matrix
Every requirement in Sections 3 to 7, 11 and 12 appears below with the tasks that satisfy it, and every task in 10.4 lists the requirements it satisfies. A script check at build time found no requirement without a task and no task without a requirement. Requirements are named by the document's own labels (F-, LS-, T-) or by section and row.
| Requirement | Summary | Tasks |
| 3.1 MVP gate | MVP is complete before V2 starts; complete means every test in 7.2 passes. | M4-07 |
| 3.1 V2 | V2 phase: features that add depth, started only after MVP is complete. | M5-01 |
| 3.1 V3 gate | V3 starts only after MVP is complete and V2 passes its acceptance tests (Section 7.4, D-67); N/A under a D-85 fallback counts as passed (D-121). | M5-11 |
| 3.2 Chat | 1-on-1 and group chat via Realtime Postgres Changes on messages; user lookup, chat creation, members (D-26, D-70, D-91). | M1-01, M1-04, M2-05, M2-07, M2-09 |
| 3.2 Formatting | Markdown only; HTML not rendered (D-05); syntax per D-30; other constructs per D-99. | M2-06 |
| 3.2 Edit/delete | UPDATE of content (edit) or deleted_at (soft delete) on messages; the server sets edited_at and deleted_at; sender only (D-22, D-76). | M1-02, M1-03, M2-10 |
| 3.2 Reply | reply_to column references the parent message. | M1-01, M2-09 |
| 3.2 Forward | New message in the target chat with copied content (D-31). | M2-09, M3-04 |
| 3.2 Reactions | reactions table (message_id, user_id, emoji); rules per D-22. | M1-02, M2-09 |
| 3.2 Read receipts | Persisted read state with a live read hint (D-23, D-92). | M1-01, M2-11 |

| LS-5 | Behavior matrix (Normal, Automatic, Forced, No connection). | M3-05, M3-07 |
| LS-6 | Toggle change applies to items queued after the change; toggle recorded at enqueue, network row evaluated per attempt (D-117). | M3-06 |
| LS-7 | Exponential backoff retry; failed items stay visible; nothing fails silently. | M2-03 |
| LS-8 | Client-generated message_id; one message per message_id; duplicate counts as delivered. | M1-01, M2-01, M2-03 |
| LS-9 | Queue survives termination; sending returns to queued at startup. | M2-02, M2-03, M3-02 |
| LS-10 | Web queues in IndexedDB; compression and Wi-Fi deferral are mobile-only (D-19). | M2-02, M2-03 |

11. Client Breakdown
This section describes each client screen by screen: the user Android app, the user web app and the owner's admin web. It applies the decisions of Sections 3 to 9 and adds only the UI rules of D-109. Screen IDs are AND-nn (Android), WEB-nn (web) and ADM-nn (admin). Each row names its phase and the requirements and decisions it implements; Section 10 builds the screens through the tasks named in 10.10, and T-49 and T-50 test the rules of D-109.
11.1 Clients
| Client | Users and platform | Purpose |
| User Android app | The owner and contacts, on Android phones; a sideloaded APK built on EAS (D-53, D-66). | The full messenger: chats, media, Low Signal Mode and, in later phases, push, app lock, Secret Chats (V2) and calls (V3). |
| User web app | The same accounts, in a desktop or phone browser (D-109); Vercel Hobby production (D-115). | The same chats from any computer. No Low Signal compression or Wi-Fi deferral, push, app lock or Secret Chats (D-19, D-33). |
| Admin web | The owner only, on the owner's computer at 127.0.0.1:3100 (D-102). | Accounts, premium flags, health checks and maintenance jobs, with no access to message content (D-103). |
---TABLE END---
One account works on both user clients at once and sees the same chats, read state, archive, mute and folders; only device preferences (theme and the Force Low Signal Mode switch) stay per device (D-83, T-04).
11.2 User Android App
Purpose: give the owner and contacts a Telegram-style messenger on Android that stays usable on weak or absent connections (Goal 1.1, Section 3.4).
Navigation
The chat list (AND-03) is the home screen. A bottom tab bar on the root screens holds Chats (AND-03), New (a sheet with New chat and New group) and Profile (Settings, AND-14), and hides on every other screen (D-142); a chat row opens the chat (AND-05), and the chat header opens chat info (AND-12).
The Android back button closes the top screen; on the forced Change password screen (AND-02) it does nothing, on Settings it returns to the chat list, and on the chat list it leaves the app.
Screens
| ID | Screen and entry | Functions and rules | Phase and source |
| AND-01 | Sign in. First launch, or after Sign out. | Email and password fields and Sign in. "Forgot your password? Ask the owner", with no reset link (D-47); no sign-up and no phone option (D-04). A wrong email or password shows one message for both; repeated failures show the rate-limit message. Offline, Sign in is disabled with "No connection". On success: AND-02 if must_change_password is set, else AND-03. | MVP; F1; D-04, D-47, D-78 |
| AND-02 | Change password. Forced after the first sign-in or a reset; also from Settings. | New password and Confirm (at least 12 characters, must match) and Save. While forced there is no way back and nothing else loads, and RLS denies every read (D-78). On success the session refreshes and AND-03 opens. Online only. | MVP; F1; D-51, D-78, D-86 |
| AND-03 | Chat list. Home screen. | Folder tabs: All, then the user's folders. An "Archived (n)" row at the top when archived chats exist. Each row: initials (avatar from V2), title (the group title, the other member's display name, or "Saved Messages"), a one-line preview ("Photo", "Video", "Voice note", "File", "Deleted message"; from V2 "Sticker", "Location", "Poll", "Encrypted message"), time, unread count (D-92), mute icon, and for the user's own last message its LS-2 status or Seen. Order per D-94. Banners: "Offline: messages send when connected" while there is no connection; "n waiting for Wi-Fi" while items are deferred (LS-5); a "Low Signal" chip while the Automatic or Forced row applies. Long press: Archive or Unarchive, Mute (D-109 options), Add to folder, Leave group (groups only); all are disabled offline (D-24). Pull to refresh runs a resync (6.4). | MVP; F2, F9, LS-2, LS-5; D-24, D-92, D-94, D-109 |

| AND-05 | Chat. From a chat row. | Header: title, member count for a group, "typing..." with the typist's name, or a count for several typists (D-57, D-118); a tap opens AND-12. Message list: newest at the bottom, 50 more per page while scrolling up (D-44), date separators (D-109), an unread divider and a jump-to-bottom button with the unread count. Bubble: Markdown text (D-30, D-99), "edited" marker, reply quote (a tap scrolls to the parent); the time shows in time rows and on tap or hover (D-143), reaction chips with counts (a tap toggles one's own), one placeholder per attachment not yet uploaded (D-90), and "Deleted message" for a deleted one (D-22). Own bubbles show queued, sending or failed and the labels "sending (low signal)" and "waiting for Wi-Fi" (D-17) under each affected bubble, and "Sent" or Seen (D-92) under the last own message (D-143). Composer: text with a counter from 3,900 of 4,096 characters (D-30); Send, disabled while the text is blank and nothing is attached; Attach (AND-08); hold-to-record voice note; a reply bar with Cancel. A failed bubble offers Retry and Discard (D-93). Opening the chat updates last_read_seq and sends the read hint (D-92). | MVP; F3, F4, F6, LS-1 to LS-9; D-17, D-22, D-30, D-92, D-93, D-118 |
| AND-06 | Message actions. Long press on a bubble. | Reply; Forward (AND-07); Copy text; react with the 8-emoji palette, at most 3 of one's own (D-22; 4 more with extra_reactions from V2, D-72); Edit and Delete on own messages, Delete asking "Delete for everyone?"; Retry and Discard on own failed items. Edit, Delete and reactions are disabled offline (D-22). Forward is hidden on a message with no text in the MVP (D-109). | MVP; F3, F4; D-22, D-50, D-109 |
| AND-07 | Forward picker. From Forward. | A searchable list of the user's chats, including Saved Messages; one target. The forward goes through the queue (LS-1) with no attribution (D-31). In the MVP a message with attachments shows "Attachments are not forwarded yet" and forwards its text (D-50). | MVP; F3, F7; D-31, D-50 |
| AND-08 | Attach menu. From Attach. | Camera (photo or video, expo-image-picker), Gallery (multi-select, at most 10 per message, D-90) and File (expo-document-picker). Each item is checked against the type list and size caps before it is queued (D-29), and a refused item shows its reason. If the OS denies the camera, microphone or media permission, the action does not start and the D-35 message shows. Group: title and members with roles; the owner adds and removes members; any member can leave after a confirmation, and ownership passes per D-91. Mute offers 1 hour, 8 hours, 1 week and Until I unmute (D-109). Changes need a connection. | MVP; 3.2 Chat, F9; D-70, D-91, D-109 |

| AND-23 | Edit profile. Settings (V2). | Display name (1 to 64), username (D-89), and an avatar from the gallery, cropped square and saved as a JPEG of at most 512 KB (D-72). The badge shows next to flagged names (D-72, D-87). | V2; D-51, D-72, D-87, D-89 |
| AND-24 | Calls. Voice and video buttons in a direct chat header (V3). | Outgoing: "Calling..." until answered, "No answer" after 30 seconds (D-98), and "Call could not connect on this network" after 20 seconds without a connection (D-73). Incoming: a full-screen ring while the app is open, with Accept and Decline. In a call: mute, speaker, camera on or off, switch camera, hang up and a timer. With the camera denied, a voice call still works. | V3; 3.2 Calls; D-73, D-98 |
---TABLE END---
Android-only behavior
Low Signal Mode in full: the automatic 2G/3G trigger, the Force switch, photo compression and Wi-Fi deferral (Section 3.4, D-28).
Local data in expo-sqlite (queue, cache, sync state) and AsyncStorage (preferences) (D-06, D-83).
Push notifications, app lock and Secret Chats from V2 (D-33, D-49, D-96).
The delivery worker runs while the app is in the foreground; queued items send on the next open (D-109).
Not in the Android app
Sign-up, email password reset, admin functions, several accounts in one app, message search and online status (D-47, D-55, D-80, D-103), and a tablet layout (D-35).
11.3 User Web App
Purpose: let the same users read and send from any computer or phone browser, with the same chats as on Android, without installing anything (Goal 1.1, D-109).
Layout
From 768 px: a navigation rail at the left (D-142), then the chat list and the open chat on the right, with "Select a chat" when none is open. Below 768 px: one column, the chat list and then the chat with a back button (D-35).
Dialogs replace full screens for New chat, New group, Forward, Folders and Settings; the right pane shows chat info.
Screens
| ID | Screen and entry | Functions and rules | Phase and source |
| WEB-01 | Sign in. | As AND-01. The session stays in the browser's storage and refreshes automatically (M2-04). | MVP; F1; D-47, D-78 |
| WEB-02 | Change password. | As AND-02. | MVP; F1; D-51, D-78, D-86 |
| WEB-03 | Main layout. | The two-pane or one-column layout above; the navigation rail from 768 px and the bottom tab bar below 768 px hold Chats, New (New chat and New group) and Profile (Settings) (D-142); the browser tab title shows the total unread count. | MVP; 3.5 Responsive; D-35, D-109 |
| WEB-04 | Chat list. | As AND-03, except: no "Low Signal" chip and no "waiting for Wi-Fi" banner (D-19); a row menu or right-click replaces long press; the offline banner follows the browser's online state (D-12). | MVP; F2, F9; D-12, D-19, D-94 |
| WEB-05 | Archive. | As AND-04. | MVP; F9; D-94 |

---TABLE END---
Web-only behavior
Supported browsers: current Chrome, Edge, Firefox and Safari on desktop and Chrome on Android (D-109); T-50 checks each.
The queue lives in IndexedDB and retries on the browser online event and the 15-second timer, only while the tab is open (LS-10, D-12, D-109).
The web app is not installable and has no service worker (D-109); it is served over HTTPS at the Vercel Hobby production address; localhost is for development only (D-115).
Not in the web app
Photo compression, Wi-Fi deferral and the Force switch (D-19); push and app lock (F10, F11); Secret Chats, which the web client does not list (D-68); plus everything 11.2 lists as not in the Android app.
11.4 Admin Web
Purpose: give the owner one local, logged place for every account and operations task, so the secret key is used only on the owner's computer and never for reading conversations (Section 6.6, D-102, D-103).
Navigation
After ADM-01, a left sidebar links Overview, Accounts, Maintenance, Audit log and Settings check; the header shows the project name and Lock now.
Screens
| ID | Screen and entry | Functions and rules | Phase and source |
| ADM-01 | Unlock. http://127.0.0.1:3100 after npm run admin. | A passcode field, checked on the server against ADMIN_PASSCODE_HASH; 5 wrong attempts lock the screen for 5 minutes. Success sets an HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict cookie with an 8-hour idle timeout. Lock now in the header ends the session (D-104). | MVP; F12; D-104 |
| ADM-02 | Overview. Home after unlocking. | Project: name, ref, region, plan and status, with Restore when paused, after confirmation. Usage: one bar per 7.1 limit (database, storage, egress, Realtime peak connections, Realtime messages this month, monthly active users), amber at 80% and red at 100% (D-45), with the action of D-123 under an amber bar. Accounts: active, banned, must change password. Maintenance: the last export, media copy and purge, each Due or Overdue (the export after 7 days, the copy and purge after 31). Settings: the pass and fail counts of ADM-08. A panel that cannot be read shows "unavailable" with a Dashboard link, and usage can be entered by hand (D-107). | MVP; F12; D-45, D-107, D-123 |
| ADM-03 | Accounts. Sidebar. | A table of username, display name, email, created, last sign-in, status (Active, Banned, Must change password) and premium flags (V2); the owner's account is marked Owner. Search by username or email, partial and case-insensitive; filters All, Active, Banned, Must change password; 50 per page. Create account and Import CSV open ADM-04; a row opens ADM-05. | MVP; F12; D-102 |
| ADM-04 | Create account. From ADM-03. | Single: email, username (D-89) and display name, with a 16-character password generated on the server and Regenerate (D-105). Create runs the shared create-user function (D-47, D-78) and shows the password once with Copy. CSV: columns email, username and display_name, at most 100 rows; the preview marks each invalid row with its reason; Create makes the valid rows one at a time; the result (email and password) can be copied or downloaded as a CSV built in the browser, and closing the screen discards it (D-81, D-105). | MVP; F12; D-47, D-78, D-81, D-89, D-105 |
| ADM-05 | Account detail. A row in ADM-03. | Identity, read-only: email, username, display name, user id, created, last sign-in. Reset password: generates and sets a new password, then sets must_change_password (D-78), and shows the password once. Ban and Unban, each after confirmation, through disable-user (D-61, D-86). Premium switches badge, extra_reactions and premium_stickers (V2, D-72, D-87). Delete account, only on request, after typing the username and with the warning that the account is erased: its messages are soft-deleted and it is shown as "Deleted account" (D-108, D-110). Ban and Delete are disabled on the owner's account. | MVP, premium V2; F12; D-61, D-72, D-78, D-86, D-105, D-108, D-110 |
| ADM-06 | Maintenance. Sidebar. | Database export: supabase db dump into BACKUP_DIR/db/YYYY-MM-DD.sql (D-29). Media copy: downloads every object of the media and avatar buckets not yet in BACKUP_DIR/media, with counts copied, skipped and failed, and Retry failed (D-74). Purge: Preview lists the count and size of the files of deleted or expired messages and of orphans older than 24 hours, by path only; Purge deletes them after confirmation (D-75). Restore drill: the M4-05 checklist with "Drill done". One job runs at a time and shows progress; a missing tool (Docker or the Supabase CLI) shows what to install. | MVP; F12; D-29, D-74, D-75 |
| ADM-07 | Audit log. Sidebar. | Time, action, target username, result and detail (counts, file names), newest first, 50 per page, with filters by action and by target. No password or content is ever shown or stored (D-106). | MVP; F12; D-106 |
| ADM-08 | Settings check. Sidebar. | Pass or Fail for: sign-up disabled, phone provider off, minimum password length 12, JWT expiry 900 seconds (D-114), Realtime public access off, the publication tables, RLS on every public table and region Tokyo. "Check by hand" with a Dashboard link where the API cannot read a value (D-107). | MVP; F12; D-107, D-114 |
---TABLE END---
Not in the admin web
Reading or exporting messages, media, reactions, polls or chat titles; per-user activity; signing in as a user; adding or removing chat members; editing a user's username or display name after creation; storing passwords; running from a public URL (D-102, D-103, D-105).

11.6 Shared Rules
Strings: all UI text is English and lives in one strings module shared by both user clients; the admin web has its own (D-95).
States: a wait over 300 ms shows a loading indicator; an error shows its text and, where the action can be repeated, Retry; a denied OS permission stops the action and explains why (D-35, D-95).
Accessibility: every interactive element has a label, text contrast is at least 4.5:1 in both themes, and the web Tab order follows the visual order (D-95).
Time: times follow the device's locale and time zone; date separators read Today, Yesterday, then the date (D-109).
Offline: the user clients show cached chats and queue new messages; edit, delete, reactions, archive, mute and folder changes need a connection (D-22, D-24); the admin web shows "unavailable" (F12).
Background: the delivery worker runs only in the foreground, and sign out deletes the device's local data after a warning (D-109).
Implementation: 10.10 maps each screen group to its tasks; T-49 and T-50 test the rules of D-109.
12. Design System
This section is the visual and interaction specification of the three clients of Section 11: the user Android app, the user web app and the admin web. It adds no feature and no backend behavior: every screen, rule and string it styles comes from Sections 3 to 11. It adds the component identifiers CMP-nn and the decisions D-127 to D-140, revised in version 14 by D-141 to D-146. Tokens are written as dot names (for example color.bg.app); one dot name has the same value on every client (12.10). Sizes are in density-independent pixels (dp) on Android and CSS pixels (px) on web, with the same numbers; font sizes are sp on Android. Instagram's Android app and website are the main UI reference for layout, navigation, gestures and component shapes (D-141); every description of Instagram is marked Assumption, and no Instagram asset is used.
12.1 Design Principles and the DoloGram Identity
Principles
| Principle | Meaning for a screen | Source |
| State is always visible | Every outgoing item shows exactly one of queued, sending, sent or failed, and the labels "sending (low signal)" and "waiting for Wi-Fi" appear in words, not only as an icon. Nothing fails silently. | LS-2, D-17, 7.1 |
| Offline is a normal state | The offline banner, the Low Signal chip and the "waiting for Wi-Fi" banner are designed components with fixed places (CMP-13), not error screens. Controls that need a connection are shown disabled with the reason, never hidden. | 3.4, D-22, D-24 |
| Familiar first | Layouts, gestures and component shapes follow Instagram direct messages (pattern table below), so a contact who uses Instagram knows where things are. Where an Instagram pattern conflicts with a requirement, the requirement wins and the table states the adaptation. | D-141 |
| Conversation first | The chat list is home. On the root screens a bottom tab bar (Android, web below 768 px) or a left navigation rail (web from 768 px) is the only navigation; inside a chat the tab bar hides and the screen gives its full height to messages and the composer. | 11.2, 11.3, D-142 |
| One system, three clients | The same token names, component names and state rules apply to Android, web and admin; differences are only those Section 11 lists (for example Enter to send on web). | 7.1 Client drift, D-109 |
| Light on the network | No remote fonts at runtime on Android, no image placeholders fetched from a service, no thumbnails that the backend does not already store. Fonts and icons ship inside the app bundles. | Goal 1.1, 3.4 |
---TABLE END---

Name treatment
The product name is written DoloGram: capital D, capital G, no space, everywhere in the UI and in strings (D-01).
Wordmark: the word DoloGram set in Inter Bold (700), 28 sp or px, letter spacing -0.5, color color.brand.primary. It appears on the sign-in screen (AND-01, WEB-01) and as the title of the chat list top bar at 22 sp or px (type token title.lg, color color.text.primary). The admin web shows "DoloGram Admin" in title.md in the sidebar header.
There is no logo mark in this version; the Android app icon is a build-time choice (D-32). The app icon used for the APK is the letter D in Inter Bold, color #FFFFFF, centered on a #0B6E69 square with the Android adaptive-icon safe zone, exported as PNG at 1024 by 1024 px into apps/mobile/assets/icon.png and adaptive-icon.png.
The browser tab title is "DoloGram", or "(n) DoloGram" while n unread messages exist (WEB-03); the admin web tab title is "DoloGram Admin".
Tone
Plain, short and literal English (D-95): say what happened and what the user can do, for example "Offline: messages send when connected".
Sentence case for every label, title and button ("Change password", not "Change Password").
No exclamation marks, no humor in errors, no blame ("Email or password is incorrect.", not "You entered a wrong password").
Numbers as digits, with the units of D-116 ("10 MB", "4,096").
Rationale
DoloGram is a small, private messenger used by 2 to 10 people, often on weak connections (1.3, D-41). Most contacts already use Instagram, so Instagram direct messages are the main UI reference: the tab bar and rail, the inbox rows, the rounded bubbles with no tails, the time rows, the "Seen" line, the pill composer with a "Send" text button, the long-press overlay and the double tap for a heart (D-141). The identity stays DoloGram's own and favors calm and legibility over decoration: a single deep teal brand color that is not Telegram blue and not Instagram's gradient, white and true-black backgrounds with neutral grays tuned so every text pair passes 4.5:1 in both themes (D-95), one open-licensed typeface that renders the same on Android and in every supported browser, and one open-licensed outline icon set. The visual weight goes to the states the document makes first-class: a queued or failed message, a deferred upload and an offline device each have a dedicated, always-visible treatment. Where an Instagram pattern would hide one of these states or add a feature, the requirement wins (pattern table below).
Main UI reference (Instagram)
| Instagram pattern | DoloGram adaptation | Where |
| Assumption: the Instagram Android app has a bottom tab bar of icon-only tabs on its root screens, with the profile tab shown as the user's own avatar, and hides it inside a direct-message thread. | Adopted: bottom tab bar CMP-32 with Chats, New and Profile (the user's avatar, opening Settings), shown on the root screens and hidden in the chat and every pushed screen (D-142). | AND-03, AND-14, 12.6 |
| Assumption: the Instagram website has a left navigation rail of icons that stays narrow on the direct-message page, with the inbox column next to it and the thread filling the rest. | Adopted: navigation rail CMP-33, 72 wide, from 768 px; then the list pane and the chat pane (D-142, D-145). Below 768 px the web uses the tab bar. | WEB-03, 12.7 |
| Assumption: the Instagram inbox header shows the account name and a compose button. | Adapted: the top bar shows the wordmark and, on Android, the Low Signal chip; composing is the New tab or rail item, which offers New chat and New group (D-142). | AND-03, CMP-21 |
| Assumption: Instagram inbox rows show a large round avatar, the name, and the preview and relative time on one line, with unread rows in bold and a dot at the right. | Adopted: chat row CMP-09 with a 56 avatar, the name, then the preview, " · " and the time on line 2; unread rows bold. The dot is the unread count badge, because D-92 requires the count; times keep the D-109 format. | AND-03, WEB-04 |
| Assumption: Instagram inboxes have a search field above the list and a row of notes or stories. | Not adopted: DoloGram has no chat-list search (D-80) and no notes or stories, and Section 12 adds no feature (D-127). | AND-03 |
| Assumption: in an Instagram thread the other person's messages are grey bubbles on the left with a small avatar beside the last one, the user's own are colored bubbles on the right, bubbles are fully rounded with no tail, and the corners where grouped bubbles meet are tighter. | Adopted: message bubble CMP-07 with radius.bubble 22, radius.bubbleJoin 4 where bubbles of a group meet, no tail, a 28 avatar beside the last received bubble of a group, received bubbles in color.bubble.in.bg and own bubbles in the solid teal color.bubble.out.bg (no gradient). | AND-05, WEB-06 |
| Assumption: Instagram shows times as centered rows between messages after a gap, not inside each bubble, and shows a message's time when the user taps or swipes. | Adopted: time rows (CMP-22) at the start of a day and after a gap of 60 minutes; the time of one message on tap (Android, web below 768 px) or hover (web from 768 px) (D-143). | AND-05, WEB-06 |
| Assumption: Instagram shows "Seen" (or "Sent") as a small grey line under the user's last message only. | Adapted: "Sent" or "Seen" under the last own message only; queued, sending, "sending (low signal)", "waiting for Wi-Fi" and failed always show under every affected bubble, because LS-2 and D-17 require every unsent state to be visible (D-143). | CMP-08 |
| Assumption: the Instagram composer is one rounded pill with a camera button at the left, the input, voice, gallery and sticker buttons at the right, and a "Send" text button in place of those buttons once the user types. | Adopted: composer CMP-10 as one pill with Camera, the input, Voice note, Gallery and Attach on Android (Attach and Voice note on web), replaced by the Text button "Send" when there is something to send. Stickers stay a V2 composer tab (AND-16). | AND-05, WEB-06 |
| Assumption: a long press on an Instagram message lifts it over a dimmed screen, with a reaction pill above it and an action list below it; a double tap on a message adds a heart. | Adopted: message focus overlay CMP-34 on Android and web below 768 px; a double tap toggles the user's own heart reaction (palette position 2, D-22) and obeys the offline and 3-reaction limits (D-143). | AND-06, WEB-07 |
| Assumption: on the Instagram website, hovering a message shows small buttons for react, reply and more next to it. | Adopted: the hover row React, Reply and More of CMP-07 on web from 768 px. | WEB-07 |
| Assumption: Instagram labels a forwarded message in the thread. | Not adopted: a forward stores no attribution (D-31), so it renders as an ordinary bubble (D-133). | CMP-07 |
| Assumption: Instagram uses bottom sheets for item actions on mobile, centered modals on the website, short toasts at the bottom, grey placeholder blocks while loading and a centered icon with a short message when a list is empty. | Adopted: bottom sheet CMP-15, dialog CMP-16, menu CMP-23, toast CMP-17, skeleton CMP-18 after 300 ms (D-95), empty state CMP-19. | 12.4 |
| Assumption: Instagram's dark mode uses a true black background with neutral grey surfaces. | Adopted: color.bg.app #000000 in the dark theme and neutral grays in both themes (D-144). | 12.2 |
---TABLE END---

Nothing from Instagram is reused: no logo, wordmark, Instagram Sans or other font, icon, screenshot, gradient or exact color. DoloGram keeps its teal brand (D-129), Inter (D-130) and Lucide (D-131) (D-127, D-141).
12.2 Design Tokens
Every value below lives once in packages/shared/src/theme/tokens.ts (12.10, D-138). A static check finds no color literal outside the theme module (M4-02). Light is the light theme and Dark the dark theme; the theme setting offers System, Light and Dark, defaults to System and is stored per device (D-83); the admin web follows the system theme (D-109).

---TABLE END---

color.scrim, color.skeleton.base and color.skeleton.highlight carry no text and are decorative, so they have no contrast minimum. color.text.disabled is used only on the backgrounds listed in its row.
Typography
Typeface: Inter (SIL Open Font License 1.1) for all UI text; JetBrains Mono (SIL Open Font License 1.1) for inline code, code blocks, the admin user id and generated passwords. Emoji use the platform emoji font (12.3). Sizes are sp on Android and px on web; line height is absolute.
| Token | Font and weight | Size | Line height | Letter spacing | Use |
| display | Inter Bold 700 | 28 | 34 | -0.5 | Wordmark on sign-in. |
| title.lg | Inter SemiBold 600 | 22 | 28 | 0 | Chat list top bar title, admin page title, empty-state title on wide web. |
| title.md | Inter SemiBold 600 | 18 | 24 | 0 | Top bar title of other screens, dialog and sheet title, empty-state and error-state title. |
| title.sm | Inter SemiBold 600 | 16 | 22 | 0 | Chat row name, settings section header, reply-quote sender on Android. |
| body.lg | Inter Regular 400 | 16 | 22 | 0 | Message text, text field input, composer input, list item primary text. |
| body.md | Inter Regular 400 | 15 | 20 | 0 | Chat row preview, dialog body, empty-state body, toast text, table cells. |
| body.sm | Inter Regular 400 | 13 | 18 | 0 | Time of one message, edited marker, helper and error text, captions. |
| label.lg | Inter SemiBold 600 | 15 | 20 | 0 | Button labels. |
| label.md | Inter Medium 500 | 14 | 20 | 0 | Folder tabs, chips, field labels, sender name in group bubbles, admin table headers. |
| label.sm | Inter Medium 500 | 12 | 16 | 0 | Badges, status captions ("Sent", "Seen", "sending (low signal)", "waiting for Wi-Fi"), time rows, pills, counters. |
| mono | JetBrains Mono Regular 400 | 14 | 20 | 0 | Inline code and code blocks in messages (D-30), user id, generated password, audit action name. |
---TABLE END---

Unread chat rows render the name in Inter Bold 700 and the preview and time in Inter SemiBold 600 in color.text.primary; read rows use title.sm for the name and Inter Regular 400 for the preview.
Bold in Markdown uses Inter Bold 700 and italic uses the italic face of the same weight; Inter has true italics, so no synthetic slant is used.
Android system font scale is honored with no cap for message text, chat row text, dialog and sheet text; top bar titles, folder tabs, badges and status captions cap at 1.5 times (maxFontSizeMultiplier 1.5) so the bars keep their height. Web honors the browser zoom and font size; every size is set in rem with 1 rem = 16 px (D-130).
Spacing
| Token | Value | Typical use |
| space.0 | 0 | Flush edges. |
| space.1 | 2 | Gap between bubbles of one group; gap between photo tiles in a grid. |
| space.2 | 4 | Gap between icon and caption; gap between bubble and its reaction chips. |
| space.3 | 8 | Gap between bubble groups; bubble vertical padding; chip horizontal padding. |
| space.4 | 12 | Bubble horizontal padding; chat row vertical padding; gap between avatar and text. |
| space.5 | 16 | Screen side padding on Android and web below 768 px; list item horizontal padding; toast offset. |
| space.6 | 20 | Button horizontal padding. |
| space.7 | 24 | Dialog padding; space between form fields groups; empty-state block spacing. |
| space.8 | 32 | Space above and below a form on sign-in; admin page padding. |
| space.9 | 40 | Top padding of empty and error states. |
| space.10 | 48 | Minimum Android touch target edge. |
| space.11 | 64 | Top offset of the sign-in form on screens taller than 700. |
---TABLE END---

Radius
| Token | Value | Use |
| radius.none | 0 | Media viewer, full-screen dialogs below 768 px. |
| radius.sm | 6 | Skeleton blocks, attachment tiles in the composer strip, admin pills. |
| radius.md | 10 | Buttons, text fields, menus, admin cards and tables. |
| radius.lg | 14 | Photo and video tiles inside bubbles, file and voice rows, reply quote (8 inside the quote bar side). |
| radius.bubble | 22 | Every corner of a message bubble, which has no tail. |
| radius.bubbleJoin | 4 | Sender-side corners where two bubbles of one group meet (CMP-07). |
| radius.xl | 20 | Top corners of a bottom sheet; all corners of a dialog from 768 px and on Android; the menu of the message focus overlay. |
| radius.full | 9999 | Avatars, badges, chips, switch, search field, composer pill while one line, reaction pill, round icon buttons. |
---TABLE END---

Elevation
| Token | Android (React Native) | Web light | Web dark | Use |
| elevation.0 | elevation 0 | none | none | Screens, rows, bubbles. |
| elevation.1 | elevation 2 | 0 1px 2px rgba(15,18,22,0.12) | 0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,0.48) | Jump-to-bottom button, admin cards. |
| elevation.2 | elevation 6 | 0 4px 12px rgba(15,18,22,0.16) | 0 4px 12px rgba(0,0,0,0.56) | Menus, toasts. |
| elevation.3 | elevation 12 | 0 12px 32px rgba(15,18,22,0.24) | 0 12px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.64) | Bottom sheets, dialogs. |
---TABLE END---

In the dark theme an elevated surface is also lighter (color.bg.elevated), because a shadow on #000000 is not visible.
Motion
| Token | Value | Use |
| motion.duration.instant | 0 ms | Every transition when reduced motion is on, except opacity fades. |
| motion.duration.fast | 100 ms | Pressed state, focus ring, opacity fades with reduced motion on. |
| motion.duration.base | 200 ms | Switch thumb, tab indicator, banner show and hide, toast exit, sheet and dialog exit. |
| motion.duration.slow | 300 ms | Bottom sheet and dialog enter, toast enter, screen push on Android. |
| motion.duration.highlight | 1200 ms | Background highlight of a reply target after a tap on its quote. |
| motion.duration.spinner | 1000 ms per turn | Loading spinner and the sending status icon. |
| motion.duration.shimmer | 1200 ms per sweep | Skeleton highlight. |
| motion.easing.standard | cubic-bezier(0.2, 0, 0, 1) | Changes within a screen (tab indicator, switch, banner). |
| motion.easing.enter | cubic-bezier(0, 0, 0, 1) | Elements entering (sheet, dialog, toast, screen push). |
| motion.easing.exit | cubic-bezier(0.3, 0, 1, 1) | Elements leaving. |
| motion.easing.linear | linear | Spinner rotation and shimmer. |
| motion.delay.loading | 300 ms | A spinner or skeleton appears only after a wait of 300 ms (D-95). |
| motion.toast.duration | 4000 ms; 6000 ms with an action | Time a toast stays before it leaves. |
---TABLE END---

Reduced motion: on Android, when AccessibilityInfo.isReduceMotionEnabled() is true, and on web, under @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce), every duration becomes motion.duration.instant except opacity fades, which use motion.duration.fast. The skeleton shimmer stops and shows color.skeleton.base only. The spinner keeps rotating, because it is the only sign that work continues, and it always sits next to a text label or an accessibility label (D-134).
Z-index
| Token | Value | Layer |
| z.base | 0 | Screen content. |
| z.sticky | 100 | Top bar, folder tabs, bottom tab bar, navigation rail, admin table header. |
| z.banner | 200 | Offline, Low Signal and "waiting for Wi-Fi" banners, jump-to-bottom button. |
| z.menu | 300 | Web menus and the reaction palette popover. |
| z.scrim | 400 | Scrim behind a sheet or dialog. |
| z.sheet | 410 | Bottom sheet, message focus overlay. |
| z.dialog | 500 | Dialog; the media viewer on web. |
| z.toast | 600 | Toast, above every other layer. |
---TABLE END---

On Android, z-index orders siblings only; a bottom sheet, dialog, media viewer and toast render through a React Native Modal or a root portal so they always cover the screen (12.10).
12.3 Iconography and Imagery
Icon set
Lucide (ISC License; its Feather-derived icons are under the MIT License): lucide-react-native with react-native-svg (MIT) on Android, lucide-react on the user web and admin web. Icons are bundled; nothing is fetched at runtime (D-131).
Every icon is referred to by a semantic name in packages/shared/src/theme/icons.ts, which maps it to the Lucide name. Screens never name a Lucide icon directly.
Assumption: the Lucide names in the table below exist in the Lucide release pinned at M0-08; a renamed icon is corrected in icons.ts only. Assumption: lucide-react-native renders through react-native-svg on the Expo SDK pinned at M0-08; checked at M4-02, fallback the Feather set of @expo/vector-icons (MIT, bundled with Expo) through the same icons.ts map, where check-check becomes two check icons offset by 4 dp.

| icon.sm | 16 | 2 | Status icon and mute icon in a chat row, chip icons, admin pill icons. |
| icon.md | 20 | 1.75 | Banner icons, list item icons in menus, admin sidebar icons. |
| icon.lg | 24 | 1.75 | Top bar actions, composer buttons, bottom sheet items, failed-status icon beside a bubble. |
| icon.xl | 48 | 1.5 | Empty-state and error-state icons. |
---TABLE END---

Icons use round line caps and joins (Lucide default), no fill, and the color of the text they accompany. An icon that is the only content of a control always has an accessibility label (12.8).
